Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 201: If Not for the Occasional Madmen.
----------------------------------------------
“Please spare me!”

The Third Immortal helped the young man, who had fallen to his knees and was pleading, to his feet.

“You’re making me feel terribly uncomfortable.”

“How am I supposed to pay you back if you suddenly demand the money?”

The Third Immortal let out a sigh.

“It’s an order that came down from the guild, so there’s nothing I can do. You know how it is, don’t you? People like us can only do as we’re told.”

“But I can’t sell my house. If we have no house, where am I supposed to care for my parents?”

This young man’s weakness was his filial devotion. The Third Immortal kept stabbing at that weakness.

“You’ll have to take them to a relative’s house or something.”

“What relative would welcome two old people at the end of their lives?”

The Third Immortal sighed.

“That’s why I told you not to borrow more money.”

The young man sighed as well.

“I didn’t know it would come to this.”

The dream of this young man, who had gone out into the world to learn martial arts and then returned home, was to open a martial academy in his hometown. As he worked desperately to earn money and make that dream come true, the Third Immortal had approached him, offering to lend him an Immortal Loan.

Of course, the young man had been suspicious at first.

But there had been nothing strange about the promissory note. On top of that, the Third Immortal had been exceedingly kind. He had treated him like a true elder brother.

He had not even forced him to borrow money until the very end. He had advised him to borrow another time, saying that having too much debt at a young age would be difficult.

Then, when the Third Immortal tried to lend the Immortal Loan to someone else, the young man grew anxious. In the end, he borrowed the money that day.

He bought an old estate and began construction to remodel it into a martial academy. But then there was an accident in which the workers were injured. Construction was delayed, and he had to borrow more money to pay for their treatment. After that, materials were stolen. So he had to borrow again.

Even so, he had meant to somehow open the martial academy, run it steadily, and pay the money back. But this time, the completed academy caught fire.

The young man truly felt as if the heavens had collapsed. And then, to make matters worse, the Third Immortal came and told him to pay back the money. He told him to sell the house where his parents lived.

“I’ll do anything. But I absolutely cannot sell the house!”

“Anything? Are you saying you’d even kill someone? Don’t say such useless things.”

“I’m sorry.”

“I’ll try to plead with the guild somehow, so let’s discuss it together.”

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

“Then I’ll see you again.”

As the Third Immortal turned away after parting from the young man, a smile formed on his thin lips.

‘It’s about time.’

Once he made the man commit a crime, that would be the end of it. The moment he dipped his foot in, they could use him for the rest of his life. Especially if he was kind, innocent, and deeply filial toward his parents like this young man, it was as good as free prey.

Ambition, dreams, hope, youth, family…

These bright and warm things were the bait that led young men into hell. Push forward with spirit! You should dream while you’re young. Hold on to hope! You’re still young! Think of your family! How could hot-blooded youngsters possibly avoid such things?

With a cold smile, the Third Immortal climbed into the carriage that had been waiting.

He asked his subordinate on the driver’s seat.

“Next?”

“He’s someone who wants to borrow money for the first time.”

“What does he do?”

“They say he is the son of the sect master of Jeonggwonmun.”

It was a sect he had heard of before.

One might wonder why the child of such a well-known sect would need to borrow money, but in his experience, the people who truly needed money never borrowed it, while those who did not need it were the ones who borrowed.

The Third Immortal leaned his head against the carriage. These days, he was so busy he scarcely had time to breathe.

The Dark Merchant Alliance was being run by four sworn brothers who had committed all manner of wicked deeds. When they began this venture, they abandoned their own names and became the First Immortal, Second Immortal, Third Immortal, and Fourth Immortal.

The First Immortal, who had first created the Dark Merchant Alliance, was cunning and merciless. Sympathy for others had never existed in him from the moment he was born.

Then, out of nowhere, he had obtained a vast sum of money and proposed this venture. He never told them where he had gotten such money. There had to be a separate financier who had provided this enormous capital.

In any case, one thing was certain. Once that ruthless First Immortal set his mind on making money, they would make a tremendous fortune.

To maintain secrecy, they kept the number of subordinates to a minimum and selected only elites, while dividing the work thoroughly.

The Fourth Immortal led elite martial artists and moved only when force was needed. If the Fourth Immortal was the fist, the Second Immortal was the foot. The Second Immortal’s role was to find the people who would become their victims.

The First Immortal decided where to use these prey. He made them do all kinds of things. Not only assault and murder, but also stealing martial arts manuals from relatives or acquaintances. If it looked as if a secret might be leaked, he killed them while disguising it as suicide.

And if the First Immortal was the head, then the Third Immortal served as the mouth.

The Third Immortal went around caressing and comforting them. If they endured just a little longer, a better life would come. In that sense, whenever the four of them gathered, they often joked that the Third Immortal was the biggest son of a bitch among them.

“We’ve arrived.”

The meeting place was an inn.

His subordinate pointed to a young man sitting in a corner seat and said,

“That’s him.”

The Third Immortal walked over to him, while his subordinate waited a short distance away.

The Third Immortal sat down with the young man and greeted him warmly.

“Sorry. Did you wait long?”

“It’s fine.”

The young man answered in a low voice. Usually, young men who came to borrow money were tense and stiff. But rather than tense, this young man gave off a dark, gloomy impression.

“What is your name?”

“Jeong Yang.”

The person who introduced himself as Jeong Yang was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“I heard you were the heir of Jeonggwonmun?”

Jeonggwonmun was a sect that truly existed not far from this place. Its youngest son was also named Jeong Yang.

Of course, this sect was one affiliated with the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and managed by Tongcheongak. If someone actually went to investigate, they would learn that there was a son who looked like Geom Mugeuk.

Like Seodopa, which had been used during the Murim Alliance’s martial competition, the Little Dragon Tournament, Jeonggwonmun was also a cover sect created to prepare for cases like this.

“Why would someone like you need money?”

The Third Immortal asked, probing him.

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“I heard you lend money without taking interest.”

“That’s right. The Immortal Loan does not take interest.”

Geom Mugeuk raised his head and looked at the Third Immortal. Unlike his usual self, Geom Mugeuk was putting on a despondent air.

He could have acted arrogant and overbearing, or he could have behaved like the scion of an extremely prestigious family. But Gowol had required him to deal with the opponent in this manner.

“How much can you lend?”

“How much do you need?”

“One hundred thousand taels.”

The Third Immortal was startled. This was the first time someone had asked to borrow one hundred thousand taels at once.

“What do you intend to use such an enormous sum for?”

Geom Mugeuk lowered his head and said nothing.

“Look here. It is your freedom to decide where you use the money you borrow, but one hundred thousand taels is truly a large sum. Shouldn’t I at least know what you plan to use it for?”

The Third Immortal thought inwardly.

‘This doesn’t feel good.’

He had just decided that he should not lend the money.

As if unable to contain his anger, Geom Mugeuk suddenly thrust his fist down at the floor from where he sat.

Even though his fist did not touch it directly, the floor of the inn sank inward.

The Third Immortal was shocked. What was even more surprising was that it had made so little sound that the people at the next table did not notice. For his age, his internal energy was profound.

“Forget it. Let’s pretend this never happened.”

When Geom Mugeuk tried to leave, as if regretting having come at all, the Third Immortal stopped him.

“Wait! Why are you so hasty?”

Geom Mugeuk acted as though, even at this moment, he was torn between whether he should borrow or not.

“Now, sit down first.”

The reason the Third Immortal held on to Geom Mugeuk was because of his skill.

‘He’s the kind of man the First Immortal is looking for!’

The First Immortal had been searching for an expert to use in a major job. Orders had even come down that if a skilled master was caught, they must seize him without fail.

“Fine. I’ll lend you the money. Since where you use it is your personal affair, I won’t ask any more.”

A faint joy flickered across Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“I’m lending you such a large sum, and you don’t even say thank you?”

Then Geom Mugeuk said in a voice barely audible,

“Where in this world is anything free? Later, you’ll use the fact that you lent me money to get something out of me.”

Geom Mugeuk glared at the Third Immortal with eyes that seemed to say he could not be deceived.

Among all the people the Third Immortal had met until now, this was the first one who gave off this kind of feeling. He was somehow unsettling, yet strangely drew people in. He seemed like someone who would cause a great incident—and that meant he was also someone who would earn them a great deal of money.

“Good! Let’s write the promissory note.”

Geom Mugeuk examined the promissory note this way and that.

“Just as I heard, there really is no repayment date.”

“If we were trying to make money, we would have invested in a merchant company. We are investing in your youth.”

At the word youth, Geom Mugeuk snorted and continued inspecting the promissory note.

“If I soak this in water, the contents of the note won’t change, will they?”

“If you’re that suspicious, then check for yourself.”

Geom Mugeuk really did splash water on it, then brought over a lamp and applied heat. But the promissory note showed no change at all.

This time, the Third Immortal snorted inwardly.

‘He’s suspicious, too.’

Did that make him difficult to use? No. Suspicious people had their own easy aspects. Once they got past their suspicion, they tended to show faith with even greater certainty. If one set out to deceive someone, the other party’s personality was no problem at all.

After examining the promissory note for quite some time, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“If you give me the money, I’ll put down my handprint.”

At the Third Immortal’s command, the subordinate who had been driving the carriage brought over bank drafts. After receiving the money, Geom Mugeuk left his handprint on the promissory note.

“Then I’ll see you again.”

After pocketing the money, Geom Mugeuk walked out without looking back, and the Third Immortal gave an order to his subordinate.

“Release the surveillance team and find out where he uses the money.”

“Understood.”

After the two men left the inn, they boarded the carriage and departed.

Geom Mugeuk and Gowol were watching the two of them leave from an alley by the building across the street.

“You did well.”

Geom Mugeuk had created as dark and gloomy an atmosphere as possible, just as Gowol had instructed.

“It was good that you checked the promissory note.”

Sprinkling water on it and applying heat had been Geom Mugeuk’s spur-of-the-moment judgment. He had thought that someone approaching with malicious intent would not cause such a fuss while inspecting a promissory note.

“Strategist Go, wouldn’t it be better to just grab that bastard and torture him? Wouldn’t he spill where the others are?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Gowol shook his head.

“The one who planned this is a clever man. If we mishandle this, he will cut off his tail and go into hiding.”

“Then what do we do now?”

“Use the one hundred thousand taels you borrowed to buy one item.”

“What kind of item?”

Gowol smiled as if he already had everything planned and said,

“An item that will give them quite a shock.”

* * *

Three days later, the subordinate returned and reported to the Third Immortal.

“Jeong Yang took the one hundred thousand taels to a shadow merchant in this region and bought poison.”

Shadow merchants were those who sold hidden weapons, arms, and poisons banned by the Murim Alliance.

“He bought poison? What kind of poison?”

“He bought Formless Poison.”

The Third Immortal was startled. Formless Poison was extremely expensive and not easy to obtain.

“Can it be bought with one hundred thousand taels?”

“It seems he bought only enough to use once.”

“Who in the world is he trying to kill?”

When his subordinate answered that he did not know that much, the Third Immortal was at a loss. Until now, the money they had lent had been used in all sorts of places, but no one had ever bought Formless Poison. It seemed the unsettling feeling he had gotten that day had been because of this.

“I have a bad feeling about this.”

He likely had not bought it for that purpose, but it was also possible that he could use that Formless Poison on the Third Immortal himself.

“How did he even deal with a shadow merchant?”

“There is a capable information broker in this area. It seems he made contact through them.”

“Damn it! Tell that bastard I want to meet him again.”

“Yes.”

The Third Immortal let out a sigh. He truly enjoyed this work. The pleasure of gently coaxing someone and leading them to ruin gave him greater delight than lying with a woman. On top of that, even as they died, the other party relied only on the Third Immortal.

The Third Immortal thought there was no pleasure more destructive and primal than this. As long as lunatics like this did not occasionally appear.

The next day, the Third Immortal met Geom Mugeuk again.

“Why did you ask to see me?”

Now that he knew Geom Mugeuk had bought poison, he looked even gloomier and darker.

“There is one thing I want to ask.”

“What is it?”

“Where did you use the one hundred thousand taels I lent you?”

Geom Mugeuk frowned.

“Did you not say you wouldn’t ask that?”

“I did. But a strange rumor is going around. They say someone bought Formless Poison with one hundred thousand taels.”

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk flinched. It was a reaction so obvious there was no need to press him.

“It wasn’t me.”

“Then show me the money. Or tell me where you used it.”

Geom Mugeuk could not say anything.

The Third Immortal spoke in a gentle tone, as if soothing him.

“The moment you bought that, we ended up in the same boat. If you cause an incident with that poison, we’ll be in trouble too. So tell me. If there is anything I can do to help, I will.”

“You can’t help.”

“Who in the world are you trying to kill? If you use Formless Poison, the Murim Alliance will come to investigate. I’m saying the problem will grow. If you want to kill someone, it would be better to hire an assassin…”

Then a name he could never have imagined came out.

“Is there an assassin who can kill the Fist Demon?”

“!”

The Third Immortal was truly shocked. He was so shocked that he wondered if he had ever been this startled recently.

“Surely you don’t mean the Fist Demon of the Demon Cult?”

“Why wouldn’t I? That bastard who deserves to be torn to death.”

For a moment, the Third Immortal forgot what to say. He felt that he had gotten involved with the wrong person.

‘Ah! This bastard is insane. Properly insane.’

If they became entangled with the Demon Cult and things went wrong, all of them would die.

“Why are you trying to kill the Fist Demon?”

“The Fist Demon touched my woman.”

“Why would the Fist Demon touch your woman of all people?”

After hesitating for a moment, Geom Mugeuk revealed his family’s secret, as if he had already gone too far to turn back.

“Our Jeonggwonmun belongs to the Demon Cult. It was a place under the Fist Demon’s charge.”

The Third Immortal nearly screamed. They were already entangled with the Demon Cult. This madman was exposing such an important secret so easily? But there was a reason his eyes had gone mad.

“The Fist Demon, who sometimes came and went from our house, saw my fiancée and violated her. Damn it! Even after my father learned the truth, he told me to endure it.”

Geom Mugeuk revealed his rage with blazing eyes that seemed ready to cast caution to the winds.

“I will kill the Fist Demon.”

The Third Immortal was making the same resolution. He quickly sent a sound transmission to the subordinate waiting behind him.

—Contact the Immortals. We need to get rid of this madman first and leave quickly.

Chapter 202: A Few Days Fly By, Right?
--------------------------------------
“Calm your anger for now.”

The Third Immortal tried to placate Geom Mugeuk. Even if he was going to kill him, he first had to settle his excitement.

“Could you endure it if it were you?”

“I wouldn’t have been able to, either. But if you let your agitation ruin things, you won’t even get your revenge, will you? Your opponent is…”

He could not bring himself to say it. To think the day would come when he would have to say, with his own mouth, that the opponent was the Fist Demon.

Whether he knew that feeling or not, Geom Mugeuk clenched his fists until they trembled.

“He said he thought of me like a son. That bastard!”

The Third Immortal had to keep Geom Mugeuk from doing anything before the other Immortals arrived.

“A bastard like that should be killed. I’ll help you.”

“Why would you?”

“What do you mean, why? Because that man deserves to die.”

Geom Mugeuk snorted.

“That’s not it, is it? You’re afraid you’ll get dragged into this for no reason, aren’t you? Don’t worry. I’ll never say you bought me the poison.”

Instead of saying he had borrowed money, he phrased it as though the Third Immortal had bought him the poison. The claim that he would never say it sounded even more dangerous. Geom Mugeuk was deliberately getting on his nerves.

The Third Immortal suppressed the urge to shout, “I bought you poison? Are you insane?”

“How do you plan to poison the Fist Demon?”

“I’ll put poison in his wine.”

“When is the Fist Demon coming again?”

“He’ll come back here in five days.”

Five days! The Third Immortal’s heart grew urgent. He had to make sure the Immortals arrived somehow within that time.

“If you get involved for nothing, you’ll die too. So stay out of it.”

“If you kill the Fist Demon, do you think the Demon Cult will leave your family alone? Your entire clan will be exterminated.”

“Let them. My father couldn’t utter a peep even after his own child suffered something like this. If that’s the kind of family this is, it would be better off exterminated.”

Though the Third Immortal understood Geom Mugeuk’s reckless fury, a thought came to him.

‘I provoked the wrong madman!’

Even so, he could not deal with Geom Mugeuk himself. Judging by the ability he had displayed at the inn before, the other man was far stronger than he was.

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat.

“Don’t come looking for me again. If you do, I’ll throw that poison all over you.”

Leaving behind that threat, Geom Mugeuk departed.

* * *

A short while later, I was on the roof of the building across from the inn, watching the Third Immortal leave together with Gowol.

“Just now, I confirmed that one of his subordinates sent a letter by carrier bird. Considering the gravity of the matter, he must have called in all the key members of Hyeopgye. According to our investigation, their leadership consists of four people, and the one called the First Immortal is their chief.”

Gowol’s intelligence organization was already showing its power. He truly made me feel that the money had been well spent.

“So that means we only have to deal with those four?”

“Yes. Among them, the First Immortal is the mastermind. He is highly suspicious and does not show himself easily. But since the Fist Demon is involved in this matter, he will have no choice but to appear.”

“So this is why everyone keeps a strategist around. It’s so convenient and nice.”

“A strategist with a chief smarter than himself has a very hard time.”

“You misunderstand. The only thing I’m good at is the Divine Art of Flattery.”

After a brief pause, Gowol said,

“Thanks to you, I settled things well with the cult leader.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his face. Compared to when he had first asked him to take care of the cult leader, Gowol seemed much more at ease.

Geom Mugeuk thought Gowol was truly remarkable. To treat Cult Leader Pungcheon so well, after Pungcheon had kept him bound like that. Though he did not know what kind of connection the two had in a previous life, he felt anew that their bond was truly deep.

“The cult leader was worried about you. He said you’ve been having a hard time lately. He told me to look after Strategist Go instead of him.”

“I’m fine.”

“What do you mean, fine? I heard that people who need help all start by saying they’re fine.”

When Gowol looked at him as if asking where he had heard that, Geom Mugeuk revealed his honest feelings.

“Strategist Go, I’m not fine. Dealing with the Demon Venerables, fighting over the succession, acting in front of these trash from Sinseonchae—it’s exhausting and tiring. But just saying that makes me feel a little better. So you say it too, Strategist Go. If it’s hard, say it’s hard. If you’re tired, say you’re tired. I’ll listen.”

Gowol gave a meaningful smile. He had said it to Cult Leader Pungcheon. That it was hard. And by doing so, he had meant that Pungcheon should say it too when he was having a hard time. Now Geom Mugeuk was doing the same for him.

Gowol raised his head and looked up at the sky. Even without saying anything, he felt better.

* * *

The one among us who suffered the most was the Fist Demon.

“…And so, the Fist Demon has become the villain. The situation was urgent, so I apologize for not informing you in advance.”

When I finished explaining the situation, the Fist Demon flew into a rage.

“Not just a villain… a shameless lecher, you mean?”

“You had to commit an evil deed of at least that level for there to be a motive to poison and kill you.”

“There are plenty of other reasons! Such as insulting him as a person, or killing a subordinate he cherished!”

“I didn’t think those would be more provocative than this.”

“Filthy!”

Since the Fist Demon had never once caused trouble involving women, it was only natural that he would jump in fury.

The Fist Demon brought his face right up to Geom Mugeuk’s.

“Was this your idea? Or was it your strategist’s idea—the one who can’t play baduk?”

“Of course it was my strategist’s idea. Don’t hold back. Go and…”

Grrrrr.

A growling sound came from the Fist Demon’s clenched fist. It was the sound of a typhoon approaching from afar.

“This was your doing. There’s no way your strategist would dare.”

“As expected, you’re sharp. Of course it was my idea.”

“Why did you make me a lecher?”

“Because it’s one of the strongest reasons to want to kill someone. Perhaps because of that, they don’t doubt my murderous intent at all.”

“Even so, I absolutely detest being misunderstood.”

“Master. I said it to men who will be dead soon anyway, so you don’t need to worry about it. The story won’t leak anywhere else.”

Just then, a question suddenly occurred to the Fist Demon.

“But did that fellow believe it?”

What he had expected to hear was, “Would the Fist Demon ever do such a thing?”

“Yes. He believed it very readily.”

“He believed it at once? What’s that bastard’s name? What is his name!”

As the Fist Demon vented his anger on them for no reason, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“It will be over in just a few days anyway. Those few days will become the most meaningful days of your life, Master.”

“What is that supposed to mean?”

“Because the name of the Fist Demon was mentioned, their leadership will move. As a result, countless young men caught in their claws will escape despair and death. If we count those who would have suffered at their hands in the future, it will be hundreds, even thousands. You’ll be saving all of them, Master.”

“What am I, a divine physician? Am I supposed to be happy because I saved people?”

“Then for the sake of your disciple, who does like saving people, please live as a lecher for just a few days.”

The Fist Demon glared holes through Geom Mugeuk, then, still exuding a dense aura of darkness, whipped around.

“Even a few days is too long!”

* * *

Four days later, the first to arrive was the Fourth Immortal.

“What in the world is going on for you to make such a fuss? Did a war break out?”

The Third Immortal was his elder brother, but the two of them got along like friends.

When the Third Immortal saw the Fourth Immortal, a master of martial arts, he felt the tension that had been gripping him finally loosen.

The Third Immortal explained in detail to the Fourth Immortal what had happened.

“So that madman is going to poison the Fist Demon to death? What’s the problem with that? Even if he kills him, he’s the one doing the killing.”

“The problem is that he bought that poison with the money he borrowed from us.”

“So what? Is the Demon Cult going to investigate whose money bought the poison?”

“If it were an ordinary poison and an ordinary target, I wouldn’t worry. It would end with: Ah, this bastard got poison and killed him. The problem is that he bought Formless Venom. How did he buy such expensive Formless Venom? And the one who died is the Fist Demon. Then of course they’ll first suspect there’s someone behind it. If they dig into the background, our existence will eventually be exposed. Do you think the Demon Cult will leave us alone?”

Only then did the Fourth Immortal understand the gravity of the situation.

“When is the Fist Demon coming?”

“Tomorrow.”

“Then we only have today. What about the Jeonggwon Sect brat?”

“He’s drinking at the tavern right now.”

“Let’s go. Lead the way.”

When the Fourth Immortal tried to leave immediately, the Third Immortal stopped him.

“If we just kill him in the tavern, his father will investigate his son’s death. Eventually the Demon Cult will get involved. Then we’ll be in even more danger.”

“Then what do you suggest?”

The Third Immortal proposed a method.

“We kidnap him and make him disappear. We handle it as though he was so despondent over his fiancée that he went off somewhere. Before killing him and dissolving him with bone-melting acid, we’ll have him leave a short letter.”

The Fourth Immortal agreed.

“That’s a good idea.”

* * *

One shichen after the Third and Fourth Immortals began waiting in front of the tavern, Geom Mugeuk staggered out.

When he entered a place with few people around, the Third Immortal appeared before him. Drunk, Geom Mugeuk slurred his words.

“You! I told you I’d kill you if you showed up in front of me again, didn’t I?”

“Listen, I came because I have something to say to you.”

Geom Mugeuk did not notice the Fourth Immortal approaching from behind him.

“What do you mean, something to say! You came to stop me again, didn’t you? You coward. I’m not scared! Tomorrow…”

The Fourth Immortal sealed Geom Mugeuk’s ma point and mute point.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been speaking, could not even dodge and was subdued on the spot.

The Third and Fourth Immortals linked arms with him from both sides and ran.

The two arrived in a deserted forest.

Only after arriving there did they release Geom Mugeuk’s mute point.

“What is this? Do you want to die?”

Drunk, Geom Mugeuk seemed completely unaware of the seriousness of the situation.

The Third Immortal handed Geom Mugeuk the paper and brush he had prepared, along with ink he had ground beforehand.

“From now on, write down exactly what I dictate.”

“What’s this?”

“Father, this unfilial son will leave for a while and return.”

Geom Mugeuk tried to write with drunken eyes, but soon set down the brush.

“You bastard! You’re going to make me write this and then kill me, aren’t you?”

It was at that moment.

A voice came from behind them.

“Idiots!”

The faces of the Third and Fourth Immortals brightened.

The First and Second Immortals were walking into the clearing. The First Immortal had arrived earlier, but he had been watching how the situation unfolded. Since he had judged that the circumstances were completely safe, he had revealed himself now.

“If he’s leaving because he’s angry at his father, why would he leave a letter? That would be even stranger.”

At the First Immortal’s words, the Third Immortal exclaimed in admiration.

“As expected! You are our eldest brother. Your thinking is different from ours!”

The First Immortal approached Geom Mugeuk. He truly had the face of a villain whose nature was written all over his features, and his eyes were as cold as a snake’s.

“So this whole uproar was because of this worthless bastard.”

After glaring down at Geom Mugeuk, he rose and said,

“Bury him alive.”

He intended to give him the terror and agony of being buried alive.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk smiled. The gloomy, dark demeanor he had been acting out until now vanished, and his expression became that of the original Geom Mugeuk.

The Third Immortal, who was watching, flinched in surprise at the change in expression that made him seem like a different person.

‘This madman really does keep surprising people until the very end.’

Then Geom Mugeuk asked the First Immortal,

“Did this whole thing come from your head? You don’t look that smart.”

The First Immortal’s expression hardened.

The other three merely watched the situation. They knew well that the angrier the First Immortal became, the more there would be to see. Of course, sometimes things happened that were frightening enough to appear in their dreams.

“Who’s behind you? Who provided the money?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the other Immortals.

“Did he not tell you either? You banded together without even that much trust?”

In that instant, the First Immortal realized it. At some point, the slur had disappeared from Geom Mugeuk’s speech.

“You… You’re not drunk.”

“I sobered up. Hearing that I’d be buried alive snapped me wide awake.”

His words were as composed as his expression.

Feeling an indescribable sense of incongruity, the First Immortal looked around. He suspected someone might be lying in ambush, but he could sense nothing nearby.

The Third Immortal stepped forward in his stead.

“Pay him no mind. He was always a madman.”

He thought they should quickly kill him and be done with it. After all, it had been his decision to lend Geom Mugeuk a hundred thousand nyang in the first place, so finishing things before that madman angered the First Immortal any further was the wise choice.

“I’ll deal with him and bury him.”

As he drew a dagger from his chest, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“You said you were investing in my youth, didn’t you?”

“You must have misheard. I meant I was investing in my youth.”

The Third Immortal sneered openly. This was the moment he liked best. The instant when he cut off his opponent’s breath. The instant when they realized they had been betrayed and sent him a resentful gaze. Of course, this particular madman was not giving him the expression he wanted.

“It was irritating, but it was a unique experience. I’ll think of it for a long time.”

“After today, I’m going to erase all memory of you from my mind.”

“Farewell, madman!”

Just as he was about to slash Geom Mugeuk’s throat with the dagger, a cold chill swept over the area, making the Third Immortal’s spine prickle. He was not the only one who felt the cold taking control of their surroundings. The Fourth Immortal drew his sword and scanned the area.

One man was walking toward them.

His long moonlit shadow was larger and broader than that of an ordinary person. Showing his current mood through an aura as sharp as frost, the man approaching them was none other than the Fist Demon.

When the four confirmed who it was, they were startled. Compared to the man who had appeared, the merciless-looking First Immortal had the face of a baby.

Then they heard Geom Mugeuk’s incomprehensible words from behind them.

“He’s very angry right now because you all believed it without a single doubt.”

Geom Mugeuk, whom they had thought had his ma point sealed, stood up and dusted off his clothes. Smiling, he asked the Fist Demon,

“A few days pass quickly, don’t they?”

The Fist Demon, his face hollow from even losing sleep, stood before them.

“To me, it was a span as long as countless kalpas!”

Chapter 203: Who the Hell Did You Mess With!
--------------------------------------------
The moment Ilseon saw the Fist Demon, he felt that something had gone wrong—terribly wrong.

His instinct for danger screamed at him to flee. But the Fist Demon’s aura, which froze his entire body solid, froze with it both his will to fight and his will to run.

Just then, the eight masters who had been hiding nearby revealed themselves.

Seeing them surround and encircle the Fist Demon, Ilseon’s frozen heart eased a little.

Gwigyeom Palsu (鬼劍八手).

Moving as though the eight of them were one body, they had once earned a fearsome reputation throughout the murim. In the battles of the murim, one man becoming two did not mean he became twice as strong. Just as a hundred men attacking did not mean they became a hundred times stronger.

But these Gwigyeom Palsu became eight times stronger. Perhaps not in actual fact, but that was the impression they gave. Their combined attack technique was that perfect.

That was why they could stand boldly against even the terrifying aura exuded by the muscular man before them without being cowed.

Strictly speaking, the Gwigyeom Palsu were not his subordinates. They were fixers sent by the Hall Master to ensure the task was carried out properly.

Thus they were his guards, his limbs that would carry out any order, and at the same time, his watchers.

“Kill him!”

The moment Ilseon’s command fell, the Gwigyeom Palsu launched themselves forward without hesitation. They stabbed simultaneously at eight vital points on the Fist Demon’s body.

The instant they moved, the Fist Demon also thrust his fist toward the Gwigyeom Palsu in front of him.

Kwa-kwaang! Puh-uh-uhk!

Along with a sound like thunder, the heavy impact of flesh and bone shattering rang out, and one of the Gwigyeom Palsu flew backward, smashing through a tree before collapsing. It was the first fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist, Black Cloud Asura.

One had been sacrificed, but the remaining seven swords struck the Fist Demon’s body.

Kaaaang!

Swords had struck a human body, yet the sound that burst out was metallic. As the fifth fist, Vajra Asura, activated, his entire body had temporarily become as hard as steel.

At the very moment the Gwigyeom Palsu were shocked—

Puh-puh-puh-puh-puh-puhk!

The second fist, Thunderbolt Asura, erupted from the Fist Demon’s fists. Thunderbolt Asura was so fast that it looked as though an asura with many arms was extending all its fists at once.

With that single move, six of the Gwigyeom Palsu were flung away in every direction. Killed instantly the moment they were hit, they slammed into trees and rocks and tumbled across the ground.

At the very moment the last remaining member of the Gwigyeom Palsu tried to rush in, aiming for the Fist Demon’s eyes—

The Fist Demon’s massive form cut through the air at tremendous speed. A single blow from the Fist Demon, carrying his full weight.

Kwaang!

The flesh and bones of the one struck twisted as if caught in a whirlwind.

The last of the Gwigyeom Palsu, struck by the third fist, Heavenly Thunder Asura, was sent flying so far away that he could no longer be seen.

All of it had happened in an instant.

To the eyes of the Immortals, thunder had sounded, something had flashed, and the Gwigyeom Palsu had become corpses rolling on the ground. It felt as though heavenly punishment had descended from the sky.

A heavy silence fell.

Because it was all too unreal, Ilseon and the other three wore blank, dazed expressions.

Over the past several years, Ilseon had confirmed the Gwigyeom Palsu’s abilities many times. When he gave the order to kill, they killed without regard for who their opponent was. Even renowned masters had died before their perfect combined attack technique.

While watching them fight, Ilseon had established a principle.

If four of them fall, then I run without looking back.

If their perfect combined attack technique was broken, then he judged that it was time to flee. But what was he supposed to do in this situation? When all of them had died in an instant?

Geom Mugeuk could tell. The Fist Demon had deliberately shown him a wide variety of techniques, starting from the first fist, for him to see.

It was clearly different from his own fist art. It was tyrannical, and yet possessed even greater power. It was not a matter of having more or less internal energy. The aura contained in the fist was more vigorous and powerful.

“Ah! That was truly magnificent.”

Seeing it once like this was better than hearing about it a hundred times.

At Geom Mugeuk’s admiration, Ilseon thought that the young bastard from Jeonggwonmun was spouting lies. The fists had not even been visible, so what exactly had been magnificent? Just who was he, to possess such divine might… Huh!

‘It’s the Fist Demon! You fool! You only realize that now?’

Those huge fists, those thick forearms that looked as if they had personally opened the gates of hell and walked out. If that man was not the Fist Demon, then who in the world could be the Fist Demon? If not the Fist Demon, who could send the Gwigyeom Palsu to the next world so easily?

“Please spare me! Lord Fist Demon!”

Ilseon flung himself flat to the ground. At the words “Fist Demon,” the remaining three also paled in terror and prostrated themselves. Once they realized their opponent was one of the Demon Venerables of the Demonic Cult, their entire bodies trembled.

Lying flat on the ground, Ilseon sent a sound transmission to Samseon.

—Wasn’t it that Jeonggwonmun heir bastard who tried to kill the Fist Demon?

—That’s right.

—You stupid son of a bitch! Do those two look like enemies to you?

—I’m not sure what happened either.

—You useless idiot!

If he could have done as he pleased, he would have leapt up and beaten Samseon half to death first. But he could not.

He had lived his entire life according to his temper. Not once had he ever restrained his anger. Once, when someone had glared at him in the street, he had chased the man down and gouged out his eyes. He never left anyone alone who provoked his temper. Yet that rage was now being controlled very well.

“Please spare us!”

The four of them begged for their lives.

The Fist Demon slowly walked toward them.

“So these bastards believed those words without even the slightest doubt?”

At the Fist Demon’s pettiness, Geom Mugeuk laughed.

“Yes. Since they live that kind of life themselves, they assume everyone else does too.”

I cleared up the misunderstanding about the Fist Demon.

“All of you, raise your heads. Straighten your backs.”

Then the bastards lifted their heads while still kneeling.

“You fools! Lord Fist Demon, touching the fiancée of the heir to a sect belonging to our cult? Does that make even the slightest bit of sense? I made that story up to lure you in. Do you understand?”

“Yes! Yes.”

The Immortals bowed their heads, agreeing unconditionally.

Geom Mugeuk asked the Fist Demon.

“How is it? Do you feel a bit relieved?”

The Fist Demon shook his head. Right, there was no way he would.

Geom Mugeuk thought that if it had been for some other reason, the Fist Demon would not have reacted so sensitively. They said he killed someone, they said he disrespected someone—he would not have so much as blinked at such things.

However, the Fist Demon was a man who terribly loathed doing such things to women. Thinking about it, that seemed right. He was a man who threw his fists toward cliffs, and a man who had never once caused trouble over a woman. That was precisely the kind of person the Fist Demon was.

“You came out to slack off and even became a lecher for me. Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk was sincerely grateful.

“I know that, given your personality, Master, you truly hated this matter. Thank you for enduring it.”

He expressed his gratitude in words like this. When one was as close to the Fist Demon as he was, shouldn’t they know without needing to say it? No. It had to be said in words for him to know. If it was not said, how would he know?

What he was most grateful to the Fist Demon for was this: that he had not hidden his dislike and piled it up in his heart, but had expressed plainly that he hated it, thereby giving him the chance to ease his mind like this.

“Among all the lechers who appeared and vanished without anyone knowing in this murim over the past few days, you were probably the best fighter in murim history.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the Fist Demon finally laughed.

Their gazes intertwined with the moonlight between them. Geom Mugeuk could feel that his face had relaxed. The Fist Demon’s pent-up anger was not eased when the truth was revealed to those bastards, but only when he heard the words, Thank you for enduring it, and I’m sorry.

That was why he could hear words like these from the Fist Demon.

“You said this matter would save hundreds, thousands of young ones? Then it wouldn’t be so bad to be a divine physician once in a while.”

As though he had said something unnecessary, the Fist Demon looked up at the moon. His broad back looked larger and sturdier than before.

Meanwhile, Ilseon was sending a sound transmission to Saseon.

—There’s no way the Fist Demon will let us live. Look for a chance and take that young bastard hostage! That’s the only way we survive.

Judging from the atmosphere, the two were clearly close. If they could make him a hostage, they would at least be able to search for a way to live.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk approached them.

When he passed Saseon and came toward Ilseon, Saseon sprang up like lightning and succeeded in placing his sword against Geom Mugeuk’s neck.

“Move and this bastard dies!”

Ilseon shouted.

“We did it!”

After glancing at the sight, the Fist Demon looked back up at the moon and said,

“You should have taken me hostage instead.”

The moment the Fist Demon’s incomprehensible words ended, Geom Mugeuk had already turned to face Saseon.

‘When did he turn around?’

It was a question that Saseon, who was pointing his sword at him, should never have had.

Geom Mugeuk’s fist struck lightly. Tap. But the result was not light.

Puh-uhk!

Saseon’s head snapped backward, then returned to its original position. Was it all right for a neck to bend like that? It was not all right.

Saseon collapsed forward as he was and did not rise again. His neck had broken, and he died on the spot.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the remaining three and said coldly,

“I don’t even like the fact that you attached the name Immortal to yourselves.”

At that sight, Ilseon realized.

‘Ah! This man isn’t the heir of Jeonggwonmun!’

There was no way the young heir of Jeonggwonmun could so easily subdue Saseon when a sword had been placed against his neck.

‘Damn it! Damn it all!’

Ilseon regretted it, and regretted it again.

He was usually extremely cautious. Even this time, if it had simply been a clash with a demonic practitioner, he would never have revealed himself. But because he had been told it involved the Fist Demon, he had had no choice. One wrong move, and the cooperation network he had painstakingly built could have been blown away.

‘Get a hold of yourself! Get a hold of yourself!’

He had already been dragged into the tiger’s jaws. Fortunately, there was not just one prey, but three. It might spare at least one.

‘I’ll survive! I’ll survive no matter what.’

He was earning so much money right now. He would live the rest of his life in luxury and comfort. He could not die like this.

“Which of you is the worst bastard?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the three flinched in surprise.

“Why are you so shocked? You picked people too, didn’t you? Who would be good to threaten? Who should you swindle? You got to choose, so why can’t I?”

As if he intended to return the price of their sins exactly as they had committed them, he pressured the three.

“If you don’t choose, all three of you die!”

Then Ilseon and Iseon looked at Samseon as if they had promised to do so beforehand. Since they often joked that he was the worst bastard among them, the decision was not difficult.

Samseon shouted with an aggrieved expression.

“No, that’s not true. I did the best work. I gave young people hope and dreams.”

“I know very well that isn’t true. You said it earlier, didn’t you? It wasn’t for my youth, but for yours.”

“Please spare me!”

“When those young people begged you like this, did you spare even one of them?”

“……”

“I thought so.”

Puh-uhk!

Samseon’s face caved in under Geom Mugeuk’s fist, and he died on the spot.

Thud.

Just as he had told him earlier that he would forget everything as of today, Geom Mugeuk tossed his corpse aside carelessly. He thought that this level of treatment suited them perfectly. He could say he would spare them in order to extract information, but he had no intention of sparing any of them.

Ilseon tried to make his final choice. He would run without looking back. Even if he was struck in the back by a hidden weapon and died, he would die while fleeing. There was a sliver of hope. He was confident in his lightness skill.

Just as he was about to turn around, someone wrapped an arm around his shoulder and stood beside him.

“You can’t run. I’m not joking— even if you started running two hours ahead, he’d still catch you. Running is what that man does best.”

The middle-aged man who had come up beside him and was pretending to be friendly was none other than Cult Leader Pungcheon.

Ilseon could not gather his wits.

‘Who is this one now?’

Not only had he approached without a trace of presence, but how had he realized that Ilseon was about to flee?

In the end, Ilseon could not hold back and shouted furiously at Iseon.

“You useless bastard! Just who the hell did you mess with?”

Because choosing the targets was Iseon’s role.

Then Cult Leader Pungcheon laughed and said,

“That’s right. There was a time when I said that to myself a lot too.”

Soon after, Gowol also appeared there.

“I searched the safe house where that bastard was staying and found the ledger.”

The thick ledger contained the names of several hundred people and the amounts of money they had borrowed.

“I will handle this ledger properly.”

Since Gowol would judge wisely and handle it well, Geom Mugeuk readily nodded.

Ilseon’s expression twisted. How had they found out about his safe house as well? He had been so careful, and then careful again. He grew dizzy. This was a natural disaster.

Geom Mugeuk sealed Ilseon and Iseon’s mute points and paralysis points.

“Only one of you two lives. The one who tells me something more useful will live. If you have something to say, blink.”

At that, Ilseon blinked rapidly without hesitation.

Geom Mugeuk released his mute point.

“The remaining funds, eight hundred thousand nyang, are in the Central Plains Money House. The seal is in my robe, and I will also tell you the password needed to withdraw the money.”

Ilseon told Gowol the password.

Seeing that, Iseon panicked. Ilseon was handing over the money this easily? No, he understood. Their opponent was the Fist Demon—how could they possibly resist?

What shocked Iseon was this. He was saying it immediately without even giving Iseon one apologetic look? Without even pretending to agonize, he was handing down a death sentence like this?

Geom Mugeuk said to Iseon,

“It’s unfortunate for you, but it looks like our First Immortal is about to shed his mortal shell and ascend to immortality, doesn’t it?”

Then Iseon blinked rapidly.

After sealing Ilseon’s mute point, Geom Mugeuk released Iseon’s mute point. Since Ilseon had been so heartless in trying to save only himself, Iseon also had no reason to hesitate.

“There is one more ledger.”

Ilseon’s eyes flew wide open, but with his mute point and paralysis point sealed, he could do nothing.

As though he had expected it, Geom Mugeuk said to Iseon,

“The ledger for the patron.”

He must have been paying a certain sum of money every month to the one who had provided the funds. A ledger recording when and how much.

“The ledger is hidden inside Ilseon’s belt.”

When Gowol untied Ilseon’s belt and searched it, a rolled-up piece of paper emerged from inside. The details of the payments were written there in full. If they had to officially crush the forces behind him, it would be essential evidence.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Ilseon and said,

“It seems I’ll have to yield ascension to immortality to our Second Immortal.”

At that, Ilseon began blinking faster than light.

Chapter 204: You Put Up With It.
--------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk released the First Immortal’s mute acupoint, money that had not existed before suddenly appeared.

“I had forgotten. I have money I put aside separately at the Continental Money House.”

“How much?”

“About four hundred thousand nyang.”

“You saved quite a lot, didn’t you?”

The First Immortal also revealed the cipher for the Continental Money House. Gowol made sure to write that down as well.

Earlier, they had obtained eight hundred thousand nyang in funds from Immortal Stronghold, and now four hundred thousand nyang the First Immortal had saved separately. Altogether, they had already gained one million two hundred thousand nyang. At that, the Second Immortal blinked with an enraged expression.

They switched back to the Second Immortal.

“All this time you only gave us a measly fifty thousand nyang, while you swallowed four hundred thousand by yourself?”

Far from looking sorry, the First Immortal seemed as though he wanted to retort, but with his mute acupoint sealed, he could not say a word.

“The First Immortal has more assets. I know he bought gold and hid it away.”

The First Immortal could not hide his bewilderment. It seemed he had intended to feign ignorance about that gold until the very end.

When the Second Immortal scored points with his gold, the First Immortal blinked irritably. A war of blinking between the two began.

Back to the First Immortal’s turn.

“I was going to mention the gold too. Count that as my merit!”

“That won’t do. If you feel wronged, don’t forget things and make sure you keep track of them. Otherwise the Second Immortal might make a show of generosity with your assets again.”

The First Immortal gave up on his wealth completely. He decided that as long as he survived, he could gather as much money as he wanted again.

“I have some land I bought.”

“How many pyeong?”

“Quite a lot.”

In truth, Geom Mugeuk had started this to obtain information rather than assets, but the two of them were misunderstanding the situation. From Geom Mugeuk’s perspective, it was something to be grateful for.

Meanwhile, the more of the First Immortal’s assets came to light, the greater the Second Immortal’s sense of betrayal became.

“Brothers? You son of a bitch! You were hogging it all for yourself.”

Truthfully, he thought it was possible to skim some off. What angered him was that the First Immortal had done it without even saying a word.

On his next turn, the First Immortal retorted.

“Don’t you realize that you earned even that fifty thousand nyang thanks to me in the first place? If not for me, you’d be lying dead in some gambling den right now with a knife in your gut. Khaak, ptooey!”

It had been a relationship bound by loyalty, but once it collapsed, it happened in an instant.

In that way, the two even revealed all the assets of the dead Third and Fourth Immortals. All they could think about was somehow getting on Geom Mugeuk’s good side and surviving. As for the Second Immortal, he also harbored a desire for revenge, determined to have the First Immortal killed.

When there were no more assets to uncover, Geom Mugeuk pressed their sleep acupoints.

The two, who had cursed at each other whenever their turns came, fell asleep.

Geom Mugeuk said to Gowol,

“Tomorrow morning, take these two and go around to the money houses first. Recover every bit of their assets.”

“Yes, understood.”

Until then, he intended to let the two live. The First Immortal might have taught them the wrong cipher for the money house.

Geom Mugeuk asked the Pungcheon Cult Leader,

“Please help Strategist Go tomorrow.”

“Understood.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded obediently.

“I thought you would curse me out by the bucketful. Saying I’m squeezing money even out of these villains.”

“Do you know how much Gowol and I suffer because of the money you squeeze out?”

“I’m sorry.”

“I didn’t say that to show off. I meant that the money from those villains is being put to good use.”

Once the funds from Immortal Stronghold came in, it seemed the work of forming an intelligence organization might be more or less complete.

“I heard from Gowol exactly what kind of bastards those fellows are. Why are you letting those sons of bitches sleep? Hang them upside down!”

“Understood. When they wake, I’ll start by pulling out their fingernails.”

Of course, it was a joke. No matter how evil someone was, it ended with granting them death. That was Geom Mugeuk’s fixed iron rule. Not for their sake, but for his own.

After finishing his conversation with the Pungcheon Cult Leader, Geom Mugeuk approached the Fist Demon. As if he had no interest in what was happening over here, the Fist Demon was only looking up at the moon.

Geom Mugeuk stood beside him and looked up at the moon with him.

“Do you know what I realized while traveling this time? I lived a life too buried in martial arts. I spent my whole life training, and my whole life fighting.”

At some point, Geom Mugeuk had once said something similar to Ian. Not to live a life spent only on training. That regret was now being voiced by the Fist Demon.

“I realized just now. This is the first time I’ve ever looked up at the moon for this long. Does that make any sense? To have lived my whole life and never once looked at the moon even for a single tea’s time.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was also directed at the moon.

“From now on, please enjoy life a little.”

“I intend to.”

If he could keep those words, if the Fist Demon could instead take one step back from martial arts… perhaps he really might be able to collapse that cliff. After all, only when life changed could martial arts change as well.

* * *

The next day, Gowol went with the Pungcheon Cult Leader, taking the First and Second Immortals along as they visited the money houses. The cipher they had been given was correct. They recovered not only the money but also the gold that had been deposited, and they sorted out and reclaimed the other assets as well.

The First Immortal had gathered this much wealth in just a few years. It also meant there had been that many more victims.

The First and Second Immortals said all sorts of things in an attempt to win Gowol’s favor somehow, but Gowol did not respond to a single word. The one who spoke to them instead was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Of course, since he deliberately chose only unpleasant things to say, such as that they would go to the most painful hell among all the hells, or that he had made a bet on which of the two would survive, they pretended not to hear him.

After nearly all the assets had been settled, Geom Mugeuk seated the two of them before him again.

“Now then, I suppose it’s time to talk about the financier.”

The First Immortal thought his life or death would be decided by this answer.

‘I mustn’t tell him everything! But I also mustn’t show any sign that I’m hiding something!’

That was the key. The problem was that he did not know much about the financier.

‘How can I package this so I can survive?’

The First Immortal glanced at the Second Immortal beside him. Still, that bastard knew nothing about this at all, so the First Immortal thought he had to score points here for certain.

‘I can live. I offered up far more assets, and if I provide information about the financier too, I’ll survive!’

After taking a deep breath, the First Immortal opened his mouth.

“I saw the financier exactly once.”

“When?”

“When I first joined hands with him.”

“Do you know who he is?”

“I don’t. There was a curtain hanging between him and me, so I could not see his face. Still, there was one thing I could tell for certain. Judging by his voice, he was a man who sounded fairly aged.”

“I suppose from now on we’ll have to go looking for a man who sounds fairly aged. I wonder how many there are in this world.”

Flustered by Geom Mugeuk’s mockery, the First Immortal quickly added,

“The men the Fist Demon killed yesterday were the Eight Ghost Sword Hands. The Eight Ghost Sword Hands would never move for money alone, so the financier is not simply someone with a lot of money. He is certainly the master of a great sect, or someone who possesses great power.”

At that moment, the Second Immortal blinked.

The First Immortal quickly said,

“There is no need to release his mute acupoint. That bastard knows nothing about the financier.”

“That’s what he says. What should I do? Do you have better information?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Second Immortal blinked even faster.

Geom Mugeuk sealed the First Immortal’s mute acupoint and released the Second Immortal’s mute acupoint again.

Then words beyond expectation flowed from the Second Immortal.

“I spoke with that man directly. Somewhere without a curtain.”

At that, the First Immortal, whose mute acupoint had been sealed, blinked and groaned as if trying to speak. It was an intense signal that everything the Second Immortal had just said was a lie.

“Tell me in detail.”

“It was the very day the First Immortal met him. I was waiting for the First Immortal to come out from the driver’s seat of a carriage standing in the manor courtyard. A short while later, the financier came out first. But when he got into the carriage, I saw him bow his head slightly to the coachman. It was clearly an act of courtesy.”

The First Immortal struggled and groaned so fiercely that his mute acupoint was released as well.

“That’s a lie! Why would the financier greet a coachman?”

“It’s true. I definitely saw it.”

Once the two could speak at the same time, things became noisy.

“He made it up. If something like that had happened, he would have told me back then. Why didn’t you tell me?”

“What was I supposed to say? That you seemed to have been fooled like an idiot by a subordinate? That the real financier seemed to be the coachman? Then what do you think you would’ve done? You would’ve beaten me while telling me not to talk bullshit. Am I wrong?”

The First Immortal could not retort. Because that was exactly what he would have done.

“You son of a bitch! What kind of trick are you trying to pull? Do you want to live that badly, you coward?”

“Coward? Where did that word come from? From your filthy mind?”

I gestured for the First Immortal to be quiet, then said to the Second Immortal,

“Keep talking.”

“I even spoke with that coachman while waiting. He was an ordinary man. He had a round, good-natured look. Ah! He had a tattoo on his chest. I happened to see it when he bent over.”

“What kind of tattoo?”

“It was a golden pig tattoo. It was so strange to see a pig carved on a person’s body that even now, that tattoo is still vivid in my mind!”

Perhaps thinking he was about to provide some important information, the First Immortal stepped in.

“This bastard is skilled at lying. Do not believe him. He is spouting all kinds of bullshit to live…”

Puuuk!

The First Immortal, who had been in front of the Second Immortal, vanished. His face smashed in by my blow, he rolled far away as a corpse.

Startled, the Second Immortal looked at me. His shock soon turned into joy.

“I survived!”

He was overjoyed, as if he might fly away.

“In any case, that bastard was the worse one. It’s only right that you die after him.”

For an instant, the Second Immortal flinched. By “die after him,” surely he meant later, when he had grown old with age, that kind of later? But that was not it. The look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he gazed at him was nothing but cold.

“Please spare me!”

“From the beginning, the four of you committed deeds that made it impossible for you to survive. Be satisfied that I’m sending you off cleanly.”

“Keep your promise! You son of a bitch! Who the hell are you? Who are you to…”

Puuuk!

The Second Immortal flew all the way to where the First Immortal lay and died.

Geom Mugeuk had not hesitated in the slightest to eliminate them. He had not hoped for their repentance, nor had he revealed his identity to grant them the grand meaning of having died at the hands of the Second Young Master of the Demonic Cult.

When he turned around, the Fist Demon was walking toward him.

“Weren’t you planning to dig out the ones behind this?”

“I was.”

“Then is it all right to kill them all?”

“I found out who is behind them.”

A round body type, a golden pig tattoo on the chest. He was someone I knew well before my regression. At times he became a coachman, at times a laborer at a construction site, at times a waiter at a tavern. He enjoyed hiding his identity. And at the same time, he was an extremely greedy man.

“To kill him, I need Father’s permission.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Fist Demon was shocked.

“Who on earth is he?”

A single name flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“Chi Sheng.”

The Fist Demon immediately thought of one person.

“Don’t tell me. That Golden Pig of the Sado Alliance?”

“Yes. That is the man.”

“He is behind this matter?”

The Fist Demon’s expression hardened. It was not because the opponent was Chi Sheng. It was not because the opponent belonged to the Sado Alliance. It was because Chi Sheng was the loyal retainer of one man.

The person most favored by the Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance, and the head of the Sado Alliance’s most elite organization, the Extreme Corps, which only took orders from the Alliance Leader.

The second-in-command of the Sado Alliance, Yelu Han.

Chi Sheng was one of the four hands and feet Yelu Han cherished most. It was said that those subordinates had sworn loyalty and each carved a tattoo on their chest, and Chi Sheng had carved a golden pig while swearing to bring in an endless stream of money for the rest of his life.

“That money was ultimately money going to Yelu Han.”

Geom Mugeuk felt as though fate had led him here. And it was not only because of this incident.

Yelu Han.

He was the enemy of one person. A person deeply connected to Geom Mugeuk as well: the Demon Physician.

When the Demon Physician performed the Divine Eye Art for him, the person he had asked Geom Mugeuk to kill someday was none other than Yelu Han. He had not yet mentioned the name directly, but Geom Mugeuk already knew.

Yelu Han had massacred the Demon Physician’s family before his very eyes. From that day on, the Demon Physician had lived solely for the purpose of killing Yelu Han. When Geom Mugeuk had heard the Demon Physician’s request, he had resolved that he would act once he was capable. Now, he felt the time had come.

The Fist Demon noticed that I did not intend to give up.

“Yelu Han is out of the question!”

“Why is he out of the question?”

“If we touch him carelessly, a war could break out.”

“There won’t be a war. There will be a disturbance and an uproar, though.”

“You’re serious.”

In the past, the Demon Physician had asked Father to kill him, but Father had refused.

Yelu Han, who enjoyed the absolute trust of the Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance, was someone who meant more than a mere villain of the unorthodox factions.

“I considered the one behind this matter the vilest of all. He hid in the darkness and used underlings like the First Immortal to suck the lifeblood out of young people until they died. In a few days, Chi Sheng will probably be informed that all the Immortals are dead. Then Chi Sheng will create more Immortals and repeat the same thing. Even if Chi Sheng dies, another Chi Sheng will take his place and do that work.”

These were precisely the kinds of men they were. The absolute evil Geom Mugeuk thought of.

An evil even the righteous factions could not touch, and ordinary people could not even approach.

Long ago, Father had asked him what he thought the demonic path was. At that time, he had answered like this.

—I think it is our cult’s own conviction in striking down absolute evil.

To catch the great evils even the righteous factions could do nothing about—that was the demonic path he envisioned. Those evils should tremble at the words Demonic Cult. That was the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult he wanted to create.

“If I were an ordinary person, I would have to back down, saying there was nothing I could do. I would have to endure it. But we are not ordinary people, are we? We don’t have to endure it, do we? The reason I trained and struggled so hard was so that at times like this, I would not have to endure. Why should we endure? Those bastards should endure instead. They should stop tormenting people and stop fucking around.”

The Fist Demon silently stared at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk silently looked back at him as well.

Considering the Fist Demon’s personality, he was the person this persuasion would work best on. Considering his relationship with Father, he was also the person it would work least on.

At length, breaking the long silence, the Fist Demon announced the end of the journey.

“Let’s return to the Cult. This slacking-off trip ends here.”

The trip that had begun suddenly came to an equally sudden end. But it was too early to be disappointed. Turning away with that broad back of his, the Fist Demon added words Geom Mugeuk had never expected.

“If we are to get the Cult Leader’s permission, all the Demon Supremes will have to step forward.”

Chapter 205: Please Grant Your Permission
-----------------------------------------
Gowol was working in his room.

He was drawing up a budget for where and how to use the funds they had obtained this time in connection with Sinseonchae. Calculating, organizing, making plans. No matter how much work he did, there was no end to it.

Just then, the person who made this repeatedly recurring hardship a little less hard came rushing in.

“The Second Young Master and the Fist Demon are returning to the cult.”

At Cult Leader Pungcheon’s words, the brush in Gowol’s hand paused for a moment, then began moving again.

“When?”

“It looked like they meant to leave right away.”

Looking at Gowol, who was still only working, Cult Leader Pungcheon asked,

“The Second Young Master is leaving. Aren’t you sorry to see him go?”

“Why would I be? He’s a busy man. When it’s time to leave, he should leave.”

“I’m going with him.”

Gowol remained absorbed in his work.

“I’m really going! I’m not joking!”

“If the Cult Leader leaves, I’ll go back with you.”

He had only said it out of spite because Gowol was doing nothing but work, but Gowol’s reaction was unexpected. He really meant to return with him.

“Really?”

“Yes. If the Cult Leader leaves, I leave too. I’m not doing this alone.”

A smile formed on Cult Leader Pungcheon’s lips.

“Even if you’re only saying that, it feels good. Never mind. Where would I go? We have to finish the work.”

“I said let’s go.”

“It’s fine. I’ve already been and come back.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon, who had flown all the way to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and back on a single word from Gowol.

“And if you go back now, it’ll be chaos.”

“Why?”

“The Second Young Master says he’s going to kill the second-in-command of the Sado Alliance.”

Gowol flinched in surprise. Of all the news Cult Leader Pungcheon had brought after entering the room, this was the most shocking.

“Yaryul Han?”

“Yes, that bastard!”

Cult Leader Pungcheon knew of Yaryul Han. He had even met him in the past.

“Even for the Second Young Master, that man won’t be easy.”

Gowol had never met him in person. But from the rumors alone, he knew full well that Yaryul Han was no ordinary opponent. Yet that was not the problem.

“In any case, the Cult Leader will never allow it.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said earlier. About his demonic path. About his attitude toward evil.

“When the Second Young Master becomes the Heavenly Demon… things really will change a great deal.”

Gowol stopped his brush for a moment and said,

“Haven’t they already changed a great deal?”

And at the center of that change stood they themselves.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk entered.

“It seems our Cult Leader has already spread the news that I’m leaving. I think I’ll have to set out at once.”

“Please take care on your way.”

“Once again, I’m leaving you behind to suffer and going back.”

“There isn’t much work left now. I’ll finish it properly and return.”

“And the funds?”

“I think we can finish things with the money collected from Sinseonchae.”

“That’s good. Let’s keep in touch by carrier letter.”

“Yes. Please take good care of yourself, Young Master. Ah, and the completed regional information network is at your disposal whenever you wish to use it.”

Just hearing those words made Geom Mugeuk feel reassured. Once Tongcheon Pavilion and Gowol’s information network were combined, the person who obtained the fastest and most abundant information in the murim would be Geom Mugeuk himself.

“Have you thought of a name for this intelligence organization?”

“Please name it yourself, Young Master.”

“I’d like to name it Eunwol, Hidden Moon, after your name. What do you think?”

Gowol’s eyes trembled. He was grateful for Geom Mugeuk’s consideration in deliberately using his name.

“I truly like it. Thank you for your thoughtfulness.”

“I’m the one who should be thanking you. I’m sorry for entrusting you with such difficult work from the very beginning.”

“On the contrary, I’m grateful that you entrusted me with an important responsibility from the start.”

While Geom Mugeuk and Gowol were saying their farewells, Cult Leader Pungcheon and the Fist Demon also exchanged a brief goodbye.

Cult Leader Pungcheon greeted the Fist Demon first.

“It was good to meet you this time.”

“I did not know the Cult Leader was devoting himself so much for the sake of my disciple. You have my thanks.”

In truth, Cult Leader Pungcheon had no one among the Demon Supremes with whom he was close. The Demon Buddha had been the closest, but that had already gone sour.

He had had a chance to become close to the Fist Demon this time, but because he had awkwardly kept a distance, they had not shared any deep conversations.

“When there’s a chance next time, let us have a drink.”

Those were Cult Leader Pungcheon’s sincere feelings.

“Hey, Second Young Master. Be sure to get permission for me.”

That too was sincere.

* * *

I ran using lightness arts together with the Fist Demon.

On the way there, we had taken our time enjoying ourselves and slacking off, but on the way back, we dashed at a truly frightening speed.

We ran without resting until darkness fell. A distance that had taken several days on the way there, we covered in half a day.

“Let’s camp here tonight before we continue. Please sit for a moment.”

With practiced skill, I tidied up the surroundings. First, I spread leaves on the ground and made a place for the Fist Demon to sit comfortably.

I was grateful to him. I truly had never imagined he would allow this matter. Considering his relationship with Father, he should have been the Demon Supreme most opposed to it, but he had prioritized his relationship with me instead.

While the Fist Demon closed his eyes and circulated his qi, I lit a campfire in front of him.

I caught game and cooked it. Before departing, I had stopped by an inn and bought a few seasonings needed for cooking, so I was able to serve him a fairly tasty meal.

“You even brought seasonings?”

“Of course. When traveling a long distance, this is the most important thing. How does it taste?”

“It’s good.”

“Do you know whose method this seasoning recipe is?”

“It tastes like the Cult Leader’s cooking.”

At those words, I was startled.

“You’ve eaten Father’s cooking?”

“I had it once or twice in the past. There was a time when I traveled around quite a lot with the Cult Leader. Back then, we truly went around fighting everywhere.”

For a moment, I imagined Father and the Fist Demon.

Two men, the Heavenly Demon and the Fist Demon.

I recalled Father’s appearance in his youth. Beside him, I could see the young Fist Demon as well. The boy who had fought in the arena became the Fist Demon, and the young man who had chosen that boy became the Heavenly Demon.

I pictured those two blunt, manly, and most battle-hungry men in the murim sweeping across the martial world. They would have feared no one and been cowed by no one.

Just as I had fought enemies back-to-back with the Extreme Evil Soma, the two of them must have fought while relying on each other.

And at night, they would have lit a campfire like this and eaten meat and drunk liquor. They probably barely spoke at all.

Perhaps the Fist Demon was now recalling his younger days during this camping trip with me.

“I have liquor here as well.”

I took out the liquor I had brought and poured him a cup.

A cup of liquor shared with the Fist Demon across the campfire had its own charm.

“When traveling, the return journey is more important.”

“Why is that?”

“Do you know when a journey is truly completed? It’s when you open the door to your room and, saying, ‘Ah! I’m finally back!’ throw yourself onto the bed. So right now, we’re running toward that final moment. In fact, this is more important than the excitement of setting out.”

The Fist Demon looked at me with curious eyes.

“How do you know such things so well?”

Because my past life had been one long journey after another. And I had no room to return to.

Back then, my room had been the courtyard of the Ghost Elder’s house. When I returned with the materials for the Great Art and saw his startled face, I had felt a very brief relief, thinking, Ah! I did it!

“This trip with you, Master, has been wonderful.”

I got along well with this upright and quiet man. Though now I talked a lot, joked around, laughed, and made noise, the person I originally was had resembled that Fist Demon a great deal.

“Ah, when we return, let’s start by reducing the time you spend participating in the Iron Fists’ training.”

Perhaps the Fist Demon had been thinking the same thing, for he asked me concretely,

“Should I participate in just one each day? White Fist today, Blue Fist tomorrow. Like that.”

“No. Then you would still have to see them every day, Master. Instead, as usual, visit all four places in a day, but participate only once every five days.”

“Five days?”

“For the remaining four days, do what you want to do, Master. As time entirely your own.”

“What about the late-night training gathering?”

“Do as you please with that. Come if you want to come, rest if you want to rest.”

The Fist Demon nodded. This slacking-off trip had changed the Fist Demon’s life. I looked forward to the result of that change.

When day broke, we ran and ran again. When our internal energy ran low, we sat together and restored it through qi circulation.

In that way, we ran without hesitation and returned to the cult.

* * *

When I returned to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, Father was at the forge.

Our cult’s forge boasted the greatest scale in the murim.

Inside the enormous forge, hundreds of bare-chested men were melting iron and hammering metal.

Sweltering heat, the smell of sweat, and the scent of iron.

Kaaang! Klang! Kaaang!

I had always loved the sound of iron being hammered. The perilous intensity of sword clashing against sword was contained within that sound. That was why the sound always seemed hot to me.

The men whose eyes met mine bowed. I also greeted them lightly as I walked through.

In the distance, I saw Father. Even in this place where countless people came and went, Father’s presence was unmistakable. In a painting where everyone appeared in black and white, Father alone stood out clearly like a red point.

Father was standing with Hall Master Gwak, the person in charge of this forge, known as the Divine Hand. Hall Master Gwak was showing Father a newly made sword. Whenever he made a new weapon, he always sought Father’s opinion before mass-producing it.

The two of them were examining the sword and speaking with such seriousness that I waited for a moment at a short distance.

The person who wielded a sword better than anyone in the world and the person who made swords better than anyone in the world were exchanging opinions. Could there be any more moving sight than this in the forge?

If a newly made sword like this passed inspection, it would be mass-produced and replace the swords of the cult’s martial artists. Usually once every five years, or once every ten if it took longer, the forge improved the swords.

These improved swords were issued to all martial artists of our cult. They were free to use other swords at their own discretion, but most of the cult’s martial artists used swords produced by the cult forge. Their quality was that good.

Just then, Father turned this way. Our eyes met. You’re back? Yes, I have returned. A short and simple, yet tremendously welcome greeting of the eyes.

I greeted Father in a booming voice.

“Have you been in good health all this while? This son of yours has returned after completing an important duty in the Central Plains.”

Then Father said to me,

“Important duty? I heard you went to slack off with the Fist Demon.”

“I have no idea who spreads such unsavory rumors.”

Of course, it was me. Whenever I went out to the Central Plains, I periodically sent news of myself through Tongcheon Pavilion. Only by cooperating well like this would I be able to receive active help later when I needed it.

Hall Master Gwak looked at us with a slightly surprised expression. Seeing Father and me converse so comfortably must have been unexpected.

“Come here and look at the new sword.”

I examined the sword Father handed me. After looking over it closely, I spoke my impressions.

“It’s slightly lighter than the previous sword. And the length is very slightly longer.”

When I noticed it immediately, admiration flickered around Gwak’s eyes.

“They are minute differences, yet you noticed.”

“Of course. I trained so much with your swords, Elder.”

“What do you think?”

“Lately, I’ve been going around punching people rather than practicing swordsmanship, so honestly, I’m not sure.”

I passed it off with a laugh. If I had to, I could make suggestions, but with Father here, was there any need?

Father conveyed his thoughts to Hall Master Gwak.

“I think it would be good to make it just a little lighter. The length is perfect as it is.”

“Understood, Cult Leader.”
