I knew well how much Sangseon worried about and cherished the Poison King. Since it was the first time in a long while that he was leaving the cult, he would be even more concerned.

“I believe that is a request I should be making of the Poison King, but yes. I will serve him well and return.”

“Today is the last time I will guide you. From now on, come on your own.”

He, who had always guided me along different and confusing paths, led me out today by the easiest and best route.

* * *

After leaving the Thousand Poison Sect, the place I stopped by was the medical hall.

At last, I was able to say these words to the Demon Physician.

“I leave tomorrow to kill Yaryul Han.”

The shocked Demon Physician stood there blankly, then staggered. I released my internal energy and wrapped it around his body. I seated him and brought him water.

In a trembling voice, the Demon Physician asked,

“How did you know it was him?”

He had asked me to kill one person, but he had not yet told me the name.

I said nothing. Soon, the Demon Physician let out an exclamation of “Ah!” He had once asked my father for this and been refused, so he must have thought I had naturally heard it from my father.

“Did the Cult Leader permit it?”

“Yes. The six Demon Venerables helped me for this matter. My visit to the Poison King this time was also to obtain permission for it.”

“Why did you not tell me?”

“I wanted to tell you after receiving permission.”

Emotion surged across the Demon Physician’s face.

“I lived only for this day.”

His was a life spent treating patients as if abusing himself, dreaming of revenge while suffering the agony of having lost his family before his eyes and survived alone. He must never have slept comfortably in his entire life. Even when he ate delicious food, he likely never thought it delicious.

I had never had a child, but if I were to witness my child being murdered before my eyes, I could not even imagine how great and deep that grief and resentment would be.

“I could kill him and return to tell you the result, but the reason I am telling you as I leave is because I know how important this matter is to you. That is why I am telling you from the beginning. That it is beginning now. I want you to take part from the very start, Demon Physician.”

The Demon Physician would tremble, feel anxious, and worry until the day I returned. He would be unable to sleep. Even so, I believed telling him was the right thing to do for him.

The Demon Physician recalled that day, the day he so desperately wanted to forget, yet could never forget.

“It was when I was young. While I was treating a critically ill patient, Yaryul Han barged in. He told me to treat the person he had brought immediately. I told him I could not. That if I stopped treatment now, this person would die. Even with a blade pressed to my throat, I upheld my conviction as a physician. And that day, I saved both of them. I saved the person I had been treating, and I saved the person he had brought.”

“Then why?”

“He said he had never let anyone who defied his orders live. He said that was his own principle. He said he would be sure to uphold it in the future as well. Since I had saved his subordinate, he would spare my life. Instead…”

Tears flowed from the Demon Physician’s eyes.

“I regret it. I should have just done as he said. Dozens, hundreds of times a day, I regret it. If only I could return to that day… I would obey him. Even if the patient I was treating died, I would obey him.”

I said nothing. Because I knew that no words could comfort him.

He led me to his bedchamber in the medical hall. The room was so narrow and shabby that it was hard to believe it belonged to the Demon Physician. A single hard bed. A small desk and one chair were all the room contained. The few books on the desk and one old lamp seemed to show the life he had lived.

There was a secret door in that room. He opened a small door hidden in the wall and went inside.

Within the hidden space, the Demon Physician’s former home had been recreated exactly as it was.

The place where his wife had cooked, the place where his son had run and played, and the room where the family of three had slept.

He was still there, in that place, on that day. Not even for a single moment had he been able to leave it.

“My young son always wanted to play with me. Each time, I said his father was busy. That his father had to save patients. That his father had to go buy medicinal herbs. I delayed it, and delayed it again. And yet my son did not hate me. That little child said to me, ‘When you aren’t busy, you’ll play with me, right? Right? You’re working like this for us, aren’t you?’ I still cannot forget the way he looked at me that day. At times like that, my wife would say, ‘Please do not overdo it. Take care of your own health first.’ What kind of physician am I, when I could not even save my own child and my own wife!”

In the end, the tears he had been holding back streamed down the Demon Physician’s face. He tried hard to suppress them, but the more he did, the more tears flowed. It was the first time I had ever seen the Demon Physician cry.

I approached and embraced him. I held him tightly and patted his gaunt back. He squeezed his eyes shut and wept without a sound. I was not embracing him as the Second Young Master. I, who had lived longer than him before my regression, was embracing a younger brother living this painful and lonely life. Demon Physician, suffer no more. I will end it for you.

A little while later, the Demon Physician calmed himself.

His gaze and mine met in the air. In a way, his face looked relieved. It was a pain he had kept buried alone for far too long.

“…I am sorry. For making you do something so dangerous for the sake of my revenge.”

I spoke to him firmly.

“I will kill him and return, without fail.”

Today would be the last time I saw his face wet with tears.

“He will not even remember that I exist.”

That fact would drive him even more mad with rage.

“I will make him remember.”

I was someone who tried not to attach any meaning to the deaths of villains, but this time was an exception.

“And so, as he dies, I will say it into his ear. That this death is your answer, Demon Physician, to that ridiculous principle of his.”

Chapter 216: Are You Afraid of the Extreme Evil Little Demon?
-------------------------------------------------------------
The next dawn, a carriage secretly departed from the Cheonma Divine Cult.

It was a carriage carrying Geom Mugeuk, Geukak Soma, and the Poison King.

And two figures were watching that sight from afar. They were none other than the Heavenly Demon Geom Woo-jin and the Chief Strategist Sama Myeong.

“I must step down from my position as Chief Strategist now.”

When Geom Woo-jin looked at him with a questioning expression, Sama Myeong wore a self-deprecating smile.

“I never imagined that the Second Young Master would make the Poison King howl at the Great Martial Training Ground. I knew the Second Young Master was extraordinary, but I never thought he could break even the Poison King.”

Geom Woo-jin nodded.

“The same goes for me.”

“And there was one more shock. I never dreamed that the Cult Leader would permit this matter.”

“What can be done? I made a promise.”

Sama Myeong knew that Geom Woo-jin was a man who did not break promises.

That was why he should have cut it off cleanly from the start, yet he had left an opening called the Poison King.

‘Could it be that the Cult Leader had anticipated it would turn out like this?’

Sama Myeong felt that might be the case. When it came to Geom Mugeuk, he could not fathom Geom Woo-jin’s heart at all.

“While this matter is underway, fully disclose all information to the Second Young Master.”

“Yes, the Tongcheongak will certainly provide support.”

“Prepare for any unforeseen circumstances as well.”

Among unforeseen circumstances, the most serious were two. That the mission succeeded but it was revealed that the Cheonma Divine Cult had been the one to handle Yaul-han, or that Geom Mugeuk and the two Demonic Venerables failed to kill him and fell into danger.

In both cases, the repercussions would be severe. The Four Paths Alliance wouldn’t be able to wage war against the Cheonma Divine Cult, but they would demand appropriate compensation, and if that were not accepted, it could result in them joining hands with the Murim Alliance. It would also be a serious problem if Geom Mugeuk or the Demonic Venerables died or suffered grave injuries.

How could Geom Woo-jin be unaware of such facts? Yet he had given his permission nonetheless.

‘Could it be that he wishes for the political situation of the murim to grow unstable through this matter?’

Sama Myeong knew that Geom Woo-jin would one day draw his sword against the murim. The thought suddenly occurred to him that perhaps he was trying to seize upon this matter as the beginning.

In any case, one thing was certain. It was not just the Demonic Venerables who had changed. The Heavenly Demon, too—though he could not say exactly how—had definitely changed. That was evident from the mere fact that they were standing here together watching that carriage, which under normal circumstances would never have departed.

‘Second Young Master, make sure you succeed and return.’

And that wind of change was blowing not only within the Cheonma Divine Cult but also toward the murim.

* * *

The one driving the carriage was Seo Dae-ryong.

When he had gone to Seo Dae-ryong last night and told him they would be departing together the next day, Seo Dae-ryong had immediately started making a fuss.

“I don’t want to! How dangerous a place are you planning to take me to this time?”

“It’s nothing major. I just need you to drive the carriage. Run some errands later, too.”

“That’s exactly why it’s suspicious! I can smell danger all over this.”

“The smell of adventure, perhaps. The smell of growth, and memories as well.”

“All of that combined is what makes it the smell of danger. I’m sorry, but I’m not going! I told you that with the Little Dragon Tournament championship, my lifetime of adventures is over. I said I’d spend the rest of my life boasting about just that, didn’t I?”

“It has to be you.”

“There are so many demonic practitioners in our main cult.”

“Because this matter concerns the fate of myself, our main cult, and the murim. I need someone trustworthy who will keep the secret until death. At least there won’t be any wicked women trying to seduce you.”

“You always strike where it hurts.”

The decisive words that moved him were these:

“We’re going to handle that incident.”

At that, the playfulness vanished completely from Seo Dae-ryong’s expression.

“Then I have to go.”

And so, with a heart determined to inflict punishment upon the mastermind behind this incident, Seo Dae-ryong came to the carriage that morning.

“But why take a carriage? Couldn’t the two of you just go by qinggong?”

“Because it’s not just the two of us.”

“Huh?”

Seo Dae-ryong glanced inside the carriage and was startled.

He pulled me away from the carriage and spoke in a whisper.

“Why are those two inside the carriage?”

“Well, because they’re coming along to help me.”

“You didn’t tell me the carriage I had to drive was carrying Lord Geukak Soma and the Poison King! Tell me everything. Who exactly is the enemy?”

“Yaul-han.”

Seo Dae-ryong blinked for a moment and asked.

“The Yaul-han I know is only one person… It can’t be, right? Ah, what kind of joke is this, going so far? Please tell me it isn’t.”

The moment he turned to flee, I spoke.

“You tell that child yourself. Tell him we went and avenged his family.”

Seo Dae-ryong turned back. With slumped shoulders, he walked toward the driver’s seat and said:

“If you put it that way, how can I not go!”

I knew that Seo Dae-ryong had been putting on unnecessary airs. He must have been afraid, but he was not the kind of man to back out of such matters. He would want to avenge the child, and above all, it was a journey with me. Since his last departure with me, he had become the disciple of Hyeolcheon Doma and the champion of the Little Dragon Tournament.

Seo Dae-ryong was needed for this matter. Even just this carriage was reason enough. If I had been driving, the inside of the carriage carrying Geukak Soma and the Poison King would have been filled with a suffocating silence.

Since the time they first boarded the carriage and greeted each other, Geukak Soma and the Poison King had not said a single word. Truly, these two were not close at all.

This had been expected from the start. And I deliberately did not try to make the two of them get along. If an opportunity presented itself, they would become close; if not, they would return just as coldly as they departed.

“I have already received all the information on those bastards from the Tongcheongak, and I plan to receive additional updates as well.”

Though I had not told them, Eunwol, led by Gowol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader, was also actively gathering intelligence on this matter. The two of them had also agreed to join us on-site and assist with this task.

“The place we are heading to now is where Jisaeng the Golden Pig, the creator of Sinseoncho, is located. I will explain the plan once we arrive.”

“I shall follow the Second Young Master’s will regarding the plan.”

At Geukak Soma’s polite reply, the Poison King glanced this way. He looked as though he wanted to say something, but he was holding back because Geukak Soma was present.

―Just one question. Why does Geukak Soma like you so much?

―But why are you sending a voice transmission instead of speaking?

―How could I ask him when we’re not even close? Did he catch some weakness of yours? Why is he treating you so well?

―If you keep using voice transmission, I won’t answer. Ask like a man!

The Poison King turned his head toward the window.

Geukak Soma noticed that the Poison King had sent me a voice transmission and looked at me. I smiled and gave him a single nod. Even just exchanging glances like this brightened my mood. Surprisingly so.

The Poison King asked something else, not related to Geukak Soma.

“Does a plan we don’t have now suddenly appear when we arrive?”

“If a strategist we don’t have now arrives, then there will be one.”

“Strategist? Strategist Sama?”

The Poison King did not know that Gowol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader had become my people.

“No, I have my own strategist.”

“You already have a strategist?”

“You need a strategist to survive in this treacherous murim. Everyone has at least one strategist who can’t even play Go, don’t they?”

The Poison King made a baffled expression. It was a moment where a harsh word would normally have slipped out, but he was holding back because Geukak Soma was present. Having these two together came with such benefits.

―Is Lord Soma scary?

I had expected him to tell me not to be ridiculous.

―Then do you think he’s not scary? My heart trembles just looking at that mask.

It was an unexpected answer. To think the Poison King feared Geukak Soma this much.

―Right now, Lord Soma is probably trembling while looking at your poison pouch.

―That can’t be. That person is not someone who fears death.

I hadn’t known he evaluated Geukak Soma like that.

―What about you?

―I’m afraid of dying. Isn’t that why I’m someone who never even leaves the Thousand Poison Forest?

Are you afraid of dying? Or are you afraid of killing?

Instead of asking what I wanted to, I asked the Poison King a different question.

“How does it feel to leave the cult after so long?”

The Poison King’s gaze turned to the outside of the moving carriage.

“What feeling? There was a time when I roamed the Central Plains to the point of weariness.”

“You, Poison King?”

“Then how do you think I earned the title of Poison King in the Thousand Poison Forest? In my youth, I traveled all across the Central Plains to obtain poisonous substances.”

Why had someone like that holed himself up in the Thousand Poison Forest?

But I did not ask. If we ever became close enough to share our hearts, I would naturally come to know. Let me hear it on the day I reveal that I am invulnerable to ten thousand poisons.

Right then.

With an urgent whinny, the carriage came to a sudden halt. Ordinary people would have flown forward and crashed, but Demonic Venerables were Demonic Venerables. The two calmly kept to their seats.

Seo Dae-ryong on the driver’s seat spoke urgently.

“I’m sorry! A deer suddenly jumped out from the bushes along the road.”

“Since we’ve already stopped, let’s rest a while. The horse seems startled too.”

I got off the carriage together with the two Demonic Venerables.

Seo Dae-ryong bowed deeply to the two Demonic Venerables and apologized once more. Geukak Soma, the one he feared most, had nearly flown forward and collided with the front seat of the carriage. Had the impact broken even a corner of that mask? Imagining such a scenario, Seo Dae-ryong’s complexion had turned deathly pale.

Meaning to ease the tension, I formally introduced Seo Dae-ryong.

“He is the chief disciple of Lord Doma. He is currently receiving Lord Doma’s unique martial arts transmission.”

Geukak Soma did not seem surprised, as if he had already known, while the Poison King was startled.

“A disciple of Doma?”

It seemed unexpected that this small and frail man would be the next Hyeolcheon Doma.

I put more weight behind Seo Dae-ryong.

“He is also my right-hand man.”

The Poison King’s gaze turned to me. At the questioning look in his eyes, as if asking, ‘Your right-hand man?’ I spoke assertively.

“Having that friend around makes me feel much more secure.”

Then the Poison King’s gaze returned to Seo Dae-ryong. Naturally, Seo Dae-ryong was burdened a hundredfold.

“You overpraise me.”

Seo Dae-ryong bowed his head respectfully. At the same time, a voice transmission flew in.

―Please don’t do this! I beg you, don’t make me stand out to the Demonic Venerables! Please treat me like I’m not here!

As Seo Dae-ryong raised his head, I smiled and said,

“He also won the Little Dragon Tournament, where the finest young prodigies of the orthodox faction compete.”

Seeing Seo Dae-ryong curse me with his eyes to stop, I made sure to finish thoroughly.

“You will meet again at the Demonic Venerables’ Assembly in the distant future.”

Having given up on everything, Seo Dae-ryong looked up at the sky with an enlightened expression and laughed. Since I had teased him enough, it was time to go.

“Now, let us depart.”

We boarded the carriage again. Seo Dae-ryong, who had been driving the carriage excitedly until moments ago, now drove cautiously like a novice driver. He was terrified of having to stop suddenly like before.

I spoke loudly toward the driver’s seat.

“If you’re going to drive this slowly, you’ll have to switch places with me. Do you want to come in here?”

The next moment, the carriage began to race forward at a ferocious speed.

* * *

The carriage ran and ran. To leave as few traces as possible, we camped instead of stopping at villages. When our prepared alcohol and food ran out, I hunted and cooked myself to treat the Demonic Venerables. The Poison King, tasting my cooking for the first time, marveled that I was good at everything.

And so we kept running until we arrived at the region where Jisaeng the Golden Pig was located. Gowol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader welcomed us at a country house there. Seeing both Gowol and the Pungcheon Sect Leader present, the Poison King asked me,

―Why is the Pungcheon Sect Leader here?

―He has a special relationship with my strategist.

―So the rumors that the Pungcheon Sect Leader left his sect leader position were because of you.

―But why do you keep sending voice transmissions? Speak with confidence. Pungcheon Sect Leader, what are you doing here? Is the great Poison King so scared of something that he acts like this?

―Is it because I’m scared? It’s because it’s awkward.

―It was fine for you to howl in front of all those people? It was fine to act bold in front of your father?

―That was because it was something I had to do. These people all make me uncomfortable.

Even so, it was a moment when I could feel that my relationship with the Poison King had advanced. Because he was honestly confiding such feelings in me.

After having the two Demonic Venerables rest and unpack in their respective rooms, I sat separately with Gowol.

“This is remarkable. To think you have moved even the Poison King’s heart.”

“Their actions are closer to goodwill or curiosity toward me than loyalty.”

“I believe that goodwill can wield even greater power than loyalty.”

“I hope so.”

I thought that my relationship with the Demonic Venerables was only beginning. Truly becoming close with someone is a difficult thing. But more difficult than that is maintaining that closeness over time. Because once the freshness and interest in a new person fades, ennui and disappointment are bound to creep into that space. I still had a long way to go with the Demonic Venerables.

“This matter must be handled more carefully than any other.”

He knew the gravity of this matter better than anyone.

“In truth, given the martial prowess of yourself and the Demonic Venerables, dealing with the subordinate Sa Sibang would not be difficult. The problem is leaving no trace of our main cult on their deaths, and likewise not causing Yaul-han to grow wary as a result.”

Gowol already looked as though he had given the matter much thought.

“I shall proceed according to your plan.”

These were not words meant to flatter me. I intended to follow Gowol’s words completely. The way to raise a good strategist? It’s simple. Follow his plan and make it succeed. Because the higher a strategist’s confidence grows, the more outstanding his next plan will be. I would simply adapt in the moment to any parts of his plan that went awry.

“Our first target, Jisaeng, is currently trying to create a new Sinseoncho. He has recently found someone to whom he can entrust that task.”

This was why punishing underlings endlessly was useless. Only by eliminating Jisaeng, who had tasted the money of Sinseoncho, could Sinseoncho itself be made to disappear.

Gowol spoke with an expression that said the plan was already set.

“They say one who chases deer cannot see the mountain. What kills him in the end will be his own greed.”

I guessed his plan and spoke with a smile.

“We shall have to teach that man who likes immortals that among immortals, there are also evil immortals.”

Chapter 217: May I Step In?
---------------------------
“Master Yang, didn’t I tell you? How can a man live seeing only profit? Sometimes you have to take losses too. But what? You asked the Murim Alliance for help because you lost a few paltry coins? Is the Murim Alliance the troubleshooter of your life? Every little thing, and you go tattling to the Murim Alliance. The Murim Alliance is busy too. Those bastards are busy lining their own pockets. So don’t ever do this again. Got it?”

But the man lying collapsed before him, the one called Master Yang, gave no answer. He was already dead. The one nagging him despite knowing his opponent was dead was the Golden Pig, Jisaeng.

He looked irritable by nature. In complete contrast to his nickname, Golden Pig, he was scrawny, and because he tolerated no mistakes, his subordinates could not even breathe properly in front of him.

“Search this man’s house and gather up everything worth money. Wipe out anyone who might become future trouble.”

“Yes, sir!”

The subordinate who received the order went outside. As always, they would make it look as if the place had been pillaged by wandering warriors, then set it on fire.

For the time being, he had to squeeze the surroundings dry like this. The reason was that damned Sinseonchae.

Jisaeng pressed another subordinate.

“Did you find those bastards?”

“Not yet. We’ve sent out more men, so there should be news soon.”

Because the men in charge of Sinseonchae had taken all the assets and run, his finances had taken a serious blow.

According to the investigation, Ilseon and Iseon, who had been in charge of Sinseonchae, had personally gone to the money house and withdrawn the funds. They had even disposed of all the other assets before disappearing. Just thinking about them made his anger shoot to the top of his head.

‘Those stupid bastards!’

If they had kept working under him, they could have made far greater sums.

He had not expected this sort of penny-wise, pound-foolish behavior from Ilseon, at least, who was clever and cautious.

He could not know exactly what they had been thinking or what circumstances there had been. They might have fought among themselves, or perhaps there had been some other problem. Maybe they had been done in by some other bastards.

The smell of money was like the smell of blood; packs of jackals were bound to gather. In any case, whatever the reason, once he caught them, he intended to tear them apart alive. Even if they had already been done in by someone else, he would kill them again.

“Find those bastards no matter what! If you can’t, you die.”

“Yes! Understood.”

Knowing it was no joke, the subordinate turned pale and ran out.

Just then, another subordinate came and reported.

“Daeho is waiting at Seojangwon.”

The Iron Sword Four Tigers.

Jisaeng had drawn in these men, who had made quite a name for themselves in the unorthodox factions. He intended to entrust Sinseonchae to them.

It would be best to have his own direct subordinates handle the work, but given Sinseonchae’s nature, that was impossible. In preparation for any problems, they had to be people who had no connection whatsoever to the Sado Alliance. Especially if the Murim Alliance took issue with this matter, troublesome complications would arise.

Jisaeng left the building and walked toward the carriage. On the coachman’s seat, a round-faced man was looking up at the sky.

When Jisaeng came out, the coachman greeted him respectfully.

“Let’s go to Seohogwan.”

The carriage set off, and the subordinates mounted their horses and followed behind.

Jisaeng sent a voice transmission to the coachman on the driver’s seat.

—Please judge whether the Iron Sword Four Tigers are suitable, elder.

His tone was endlessly courteous and respectful.

The person pretending to be the coachman was the real Golden Pig, Jisaeng.

In the past, Iseon had told Geom Mugeuk that he had seen a tattoo on Jisaeng’s coachman, and that person was this very coachman.

—By now, do you not have a good eye for people yourself?

—I have not learned even one-hundredth of your discernment, elder.

—Excessive humility is flattery.

—I apologize, elder.

—Trust your own eyes and decide.

The man had already lived as the fake Jisaeng for over ten years. He thought like Jisaeng and lived like Jisaeng. At times, he even felt as if he himself were the real Jisaeng.

During all those years, there had been no crisis severe enough to threaten his life. One might think that by now he could reveal himself and live openly, but the real Jisaeng lived while hiding himself thoroughly.

The man thought Jisaeng was truly remarkable. Even just from what he knew, Jisaeng had amassed a tremendous fortune he could not spend in a lifetime. If it were him, he would never be able to wear those shabby clothes and play coachman.

—How are this month’s finances?

—We are still short twenty thousand taels for the money we must send to the higher-ups.

He could not make a report saying that because this or that had happened, they could not send up the tribute this month.

That is your problem.

That had always been the higher-ups’ stance.

—Do not worry. One way or another, I will handle it so that neither the higher-ups nor you, elder, are affected.

—Do so.

Whenever something like this happened, the real Jisaeng had never once said, I’ll cover it with my own money this month. Usually, the man thought he was truly incredible, but when he saw aspects like this, he also felt disappointed. Did one really have to be this obsessed with money?

Then again, in a sense, even that was remarkable. He was a man with a firm philosophy of life when it came to money. He lived for the fun of earning and gathering money. To him, money was everything.

So he had once asked.

“Why do you not become independent?”

It was because he thought that with the real Jisaeng’s mind, he would amass enormous wealth wherever he went. As things stood now, more than two-thirds of the money he earned was offered up to Yaryul Han.

At that time, the real Jisaeng had answered like this.

“No matter how much money I earn, in the end, someone like me will take it all from me.”

He meant that in the end, someone with powerful backing would seize it.

“When greed becomes excessive, it turns into disaster.”

Hearing the greediest person he knew say such a thing left the fake Jisaeng with a strange feeling. If greed truly became disaster when excessive, then he himself would be disaster incarnate.

The carriage arrived at Seohogwan.

The fake Jisaeng and the subordinates went inside first, and the real Jisaeng got down from the carriage. In truth, this moment was the one he liked most after making money.

Peering at the world while hiding his identity.

The strange pleasure that came from no one recognizing his true face. The joy, as if he had become a god, of manipulating humans like pieces on a chessboard.

With his hands clasped behind his back, the real Jisaeng began strolling around the courtyard.

* * *

The one waiting for Jisaeng in the reception hall was Daeho, the eldest of the Iron Sword Four Tigers.

Between them hung a blind set up by the subordinates. It was a special blind that allowed Jisaeng’s side to see the other side, while preventing the other side from seeing Jisaeng.

“I am honored to meet you, honored sir.”

The moment when the fake Jisaeng had to become the real Jisaeng.

This fake, too, was feeling a strange pleasure. It was the moment he became the grand figure known as Jisaeng, one of the four men serving Yaryul Han, the second-in-command of the Sado Alliance.

No matter who they were, they bowed their heads before him, and he hoped the pleasure granted by power would last forever.

“You’ve worked hard coming all this way.”

“If you call for me, elder, I ought to run ten thousand li in a single breath.”

Looking at the man seated before him, Jisaeng was inwardly pleased.

‘Perfect.’

From the beginning, he had been the one Jisaeng liked best among several candidates. In Jisaeng’s eyes, he looked nothing like a tiger and every bit like a wild dog. That was why he was perfect.

Someone of sound mind could not carry out work like Sinseonchae. It was work only those who felt not the slightest pang of guilt even after destroying a person’s soul could do. Therefore, it was not work for tigers, but for wild dogs.

In that respect, the Iron Sword Four Tigers were suitable in many ways. Their past lives proved it. They had committed all manner of wicked deeds that were truly difficult even to put into words.

“I’ll be frank with you. The men who handled this work before harbored other intentions, so I dealt with them myself.”

The fact that those bastards had run off with the money was something that absolutely must not become known.

“Betrayal means death.”

“I will offer my loyalty until the day I die.”

“Once the work begins in earnest, even mentioning our alliance means death.”

“I will keep that in mind.”

“As long as you keep those two things, you will be able to make a great deal of money.”

“I will work as hard as an ox.”

Daeho smiled. His smile revealed a vulgarity that could never be hidden.

“And before long, I would like to see your brothers as well.”

“They came with me. Contact me whenever you wish.”

“I will send word soon.”

“Understood.”

After bowing respectfully like that, Daeho withdrew.

After he left Seohogwan, the fake Jisaeng came out of the building and climbed into the carriage.

—What do you think?

—Judging by his physiognomy, he is full of greed and pettiness, but at the very least he does not have the look of someone who will betray easily. He seems suitable for this matter.

—Then I will proceed.

Thus the carriage, too, left the place.

* * *

Daeho returned to his lodgings.

He was in a good mood. Though he had always gone around saying he had been born a villain, he had been growing sick of that villainy. Just when he had been thinking he should pull off one big job and retire, Jisaeng had chosen them.

“You bastards! It’s done!”

The very moment Daeho opened the door and stepped inside, he flinched in shock.

Iho, Samho, and Saho were sitting asleep on chairs lined up side by side.

And among them sat a young man he had never seen before.

At first, he thought it was a young man his younger brothers had called over.

‘I told them not to bring in outsiders.’

But immediately, another thought arose. They had called someone over and then fallen asleep? Or were they trying to tease him? Doing something they had never done in their lives?

These thoughts flashed past in an instant.

Daeho’s hesitation did not last long. Just as he turned with lightning speed to flee, it happened.

Daeho’s entire body froze.

At some point, a man wearing a white mask had appeared behind him. The moment his gaze met the eyes within the mask, cold as sheets of ice, his body froze stiff. He had met all kinds of evildoers, but this was the first time he had ever seen eyes so terrifying.

Geugak Soma slowly reached out and lightly sealed Daeho’s internal energy. Though Daeho had rolled around the murim as much as anyone, he could not so much as twitch a finger even as the other man touched the acupoints around his dantian.

Behind Geugak Soma, two more men entered. They were none other than the Poison King and Seo Daeryong.

“I’m not going to interfere. I’ll just watch.”

The Poison King walked past Daeho and sat on the desk, while Seo Daeryong closed the door.

Geom Mugeuk called to Daeho.

“Come closer.”

Daeho slowly walked toward Geom Mugeuk.

‘I have to keep my wits about me if I want to live!’

That was his only thought.

When he came near Geom Mugeuk, he was finally able to look closely at the others, Iho, Samho, and Saho. Their bodies had already gone rigid.

‘They’re all dead!’

Daeho’s heart sank. There were no wounds on their bodies, but they looked as if they had been taken down without even drawing their swords. Who on earth were these people?

“Do you know who we are, doing this?”

“The Iron Sword Four Tigers. You’re the eldest, Daeho.”

When he answered accurately, Daeho quickly said,

“We’ve been entrusted with an important task. You’ve made a grave mistake.”

“You mean taking charge of Sinseonchae for the Sado Alliance? What’s so impressive about that?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Daeho was startled.

“You knew who was behind us and still did this?”

At that moment, Geugak Soma walked forward, stared intently at Daeho, and said,

“There’s no need to waste time. Give me just half a quarter-hour. I’ll turn him into an obedient dog.”

The hairs all over Daeho’s body stood on end at once, and his entire body trembled.

Then the Poison King spoke politely to Geugak Soma.

“May I step in for a moment?”

Geugak Soma looked at the Poison King. The Poison King could not meet Geugak Soma’s eyes.

Geugak Soma readily nodded and took a step back.

“By all means.”

It was the first moment the two had shared an understanding since leaving the main cult.

The Poison King rummaged through his bag and took out a medicine bottle as he said to Geom Mugeuk,

“How can you trust that kind of human and proceed with the work? On the other hand, you can trust gu poison without question.”

Inside the medicine bottle, a hideous insect was wriggling.

“Once this thing settles in his brain, he’ll cut off his own arm if you tell him to.”

At those words, Daeho went ashen. Just imagining that repulsive thing entering his head already made him want to vomit.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and asked the Poison King,

“Didn’t you say you weren’t going to step in?”

“Now that I’ve actually seen him, I want to test it. It’s been quite a while since I tested it on a person.”

“Unfortunately, we don’t have time to test gu poison.”

“Then feed him this, at least.”

The Poison King took out another poison bottle from his bag.

“It’s a poison that will make all the organs in his body melt down and kill him if he doesn’t take the antidote every day. Tell him to try betraying us if he wants.”

“It must leave no poison behind later.”

“Whose poison do you think this is, for you to worry about that?”

“Then this is perfect!”

The next moment, Daeho cried out urgently.

“Please spare me! What do you want me to do?”

The four men’s gazes turned toward Daeho. Daeho’s entire body was trembling. It was a bizarre combination that seemed neither villainous nor righteous.

Until now, he had experienced all manner of evildoers, but this was the first time he had encountered people who evoked such primal fear. They seemed like opponents against whom no trick would ever work.

Geom Mugeuk approached him and spoke in a gentle tone.

“Big brother, I’ll be in your care from now on.”

The moment he heard those words, Daeho understood. These people intended to play the role of his brothers. That was also why they had already killed all three of his younger brothers.

Geom Mugeuk held out the Poison King’s poison to him.

“Big brother, drink this first, and then let’s talk about what you need to do.”

“You don’t need to make me drink it! If you just spare me, I’ll do anything you tell me! I swear on my name, on my parents—no, I swear to heaven and earth! If I don’t keep my word, may heavenly punishment strike me dead!”

At that, Geom Mugeuk pried open his mouth and poured in the medicine.

“I don’t believe you. Now, drink it all down!”

Chapter 218: You Invited Me Over and Served Cheap Liquor.
---------------------------------------------------------
A searing liquid slid down Daeho’s throat.

It was a taste he had never experienced before. The instant he swallowed it, Daeho realized that he had consumed something he absolutely should not have.

The Poison King approached and handed him a black pill.

“Chew it thoroughly before swallowing. Otherwise, your insides will melt.”

Startled, Daeho hurriedly swallowed the pill. As the antidote took effect, he felt a cooling sensation within his stomach.

“Until I completely purge the poison from you, you must take this antidote once a day without fail. For every hour you’re late, another organ will melt. The last one to melt will be your heart.”

Daeho’s vision went dark. Yet, at the same time, a thread of hope remained. They must have kept him alive because he had some value to them.

“What do I have to do now?”

“You’ll take us to Jisaeng. That man over there is Number Two, I’m Number Three, and that gentleman over there is Number Four.”

The first person Geom Mugeuk indicated when speaking to Daeho was Seo Daeryong, while the last was the Poison King.

The Poison King asked me with a startled expression,

“Why am I Number Four?”

“We need three people, but as you can see, you’re wearing a mask and can’t go out.”

I deliberately refrained from calling him Soma in front of Daeho.

The Poison King and the Extreme Evil Demon’s eyes met again.

“Given the circumstances, I ask this of you.”

It was not something the Extreme Evil Demon should have needed to ask, but he spoke politely to the Poison King.

Just as the Extreme Evil Demon had willingly stepped aside earlier, the Poison King readily accepted the request.

“Very well. I’ll do it.”

At that moment, Daeho said cautiously,

“They all look too young. I’m not sure he’ll believe us.”

Geom Mugeuk then said something meaningful.

“The key to this operation is making sure he doesn’t believe us.”

Daeho could not understand what he meant. Still, he asked the one question that had been bothering him all along.

“Since I’ve already been poisoned, let me ask just one thing. Who in the world are you people?”

“Who are we? We’re merely the younger brothers our leader cherishes.”

When Geom Mugeuk pressed Daeho’s acupoint, Daeho collapsed and fell asleep.

After putting him to sleep, Geom Mugeuk spoke to the two Demon Venerables.

“Military Advisor Go is proceeding with the operation, so Jisaeng should contact us within a few days. We dangled a dazzling, glittering bait that he absolutely cannot refuse.”

This operation was proceeding exactly according to Goweol’s plan. Geom Mugeuk had told Goweol not to feel burdened and to make his plans at ease.

Goweol’s enemy was not the Evil Path Alliance or Jisaeng. It was the pressure he felt as a military advisor to carry out his duties properly and earn Geom Mugeuk’s approval.

Seo Daeryong tucked the corpses of the dead Iron Sword Four Tigers beneath his arms.

“I’ll dispose of the bodies.”

“I’ll help.”

Geom Mugeuk tucked the remaining corpse beneath his arm and followed him outside.

Together with Seo Daeryong, he dug a deep pit in the backyard and buried the three men. Considering the evil deeds they had committed, they should have thrown the bodies into the hills to be devoured by wild beasts. But for the moment, they had to impersonate these men, so that was impossible.

“As expected, whenever I go out with you, Hall Master, corpses come pouring out.”

“It may be because of you.”

“Because of me?”

“When we first met, you said that the Cult would never change. Those words made me steel my resolve. I swore I would make it change.”

Seo Daeryong stared silently at Geom Mugeuk before speaking in a low voice.

“I gave you only one, yet Hall Master, you’ve repaid me with ten.”

He meant that Geom Mugeuk’s words had given him that much gratitude and strength.

If someone knew how to appreciate kindness like that, Geom Mugeuk could give him not ten, but a hundred. That was what he felt.

Looking at the bodies in the pit, Seo Daeryong said,

“I’m sorry. They died because of me.”

After Seo Daeryong covered the bodies with earth, he stamped down the ground with his foot. Once they had concealed the site so it would not draw attention, Seo Daeryong said to me,

“Aren’t you coming in?”

“We’ll go in later. Give those two some time to grow closer.”

Looking at the building, Seo Daeryong said,

“Come to think of it, the two most frightening people in the Cult are in there.”

Seo Daeryong was secretly curious. How would Geom Mugeuk describe those two people?

* * *

The Extreme Evil Demon was looking out the window, while the Poison King pointlessly reorganized the poisons in his bag.

Then the two of them spoke at the same time.

“Poison King…”

“Soma…”

“Ah, please, go ahead.”

“No, you go ahead.”

Silence followed, until the Poison King spoke first.

“I never expected that I would end up in a place like this with you.”

“Neither did I.”

“Do you know something? Whenever Second Young Master talks about the Demon Venerables, his eyes shine the brightest when he mentions you, Soma. You could say he gets excited. That’s the impression I get.”

Perhaps because he had heard something pleasant, the eyes within the Extreme Evil Demon’s eye sockets smiled happily.

“I feel the same. I think I get excited whenever I’m with Second Young Master, too. I don’t know why.”

He would never know. He would never know that, in Geom Mugeuk’s life before his regression, he had wanted to make Geom Mugeuk his friend. That he had been the only person to die by Geom Mugeuk’s sword after showing him his face. He would never know that the deep bond between them had existed since before the regression.

The Poison King, on the other hand, felt something different.

“I feel uneasy whenever I see Second Young Master. As if he were a poison without an antidote.”

It meant that once one was poisoned, there was no remedy. His words also carried the implication that he did not want to fall into such a relationship.

“When we make poison, we consider poison without an antidote a failure.”

The Extreme Evil Demon’s gaze turned back toward the window. In the distant courtyard, the poison without an antidote was walking this way while speaking with Seo Daeryong.

In a voice so quiet it could barely be heard, the Extreme Evil Demon said,

“I don’t mind.”

* * *

Several days later, Jisaeng received a report from one of his subordinates.

“I heard a strange rumor about the Iron Sword Four Tigers.”

“What rumor?”

“Apparently, about a month before they came here, they raided a caravan and obtained three top-grade Night Pearls.”

At the mention of top-grade Night Pearls, Jisaeng was startled. They were extraordinarily expensive, and so rare that even those with money could not easily acquire them. The wealthy men of the martial world bought them up the moment they appeared. And the Iron Sword Four Tigers had obtained three?

“There’s even a rumor that several of the Iron Sword Four Tigers died in the process.”

“When we investigated them beforehand, there was no mention of anything like that.”

There had been several candidates to entrust with Sinseonchae. Jisaeng had investigated them thoroughly, and that was why he had ultimately chosen the Iron Sword Four Tigers.

“Perhaps it happened recently.”

Even so, Daeho had not shown the slightest sign of it. When he had said he wanted to see his brothers’ faces, hadn’t Daeho offered to show them at any time? If several of the four had died, then that meant everyone except Daeho was dead.

“It won’t be difficult to confirm. We had to meet them once anyway. Go and tell the Iron Sword Four Tigers that I wish to see them.”

Half an hour later, Jisaeng met the Iron Sword Four Tigers again at the manor where they had met last time.

As before, a screen had been lowered before Jisaeng, allowing only those on the other side to see his face.

“I greet the elder. These are my younger brothers. Come, greet him.”

At Daeho’s words, the three men removed their bamboo hats. The moment Jisaeng saw them, he flinched.

The young man to Daeho’s left was handsome. His eyes were clear and deep, making it difficult to believe that he was one of the Iron Sword Four Tigers, men who had lived by committing evil deeds. The man on the other side was small and slight, which also did not suit the Iron Sword Four Tigers. The last man looked far too young to be one of them—he seemed to have only just entered the martial world.

“You’re younger than I expected.”

“Wouldn’t youth make us more passionate in our work?”

Geom Mugeuk, the Poison King, and Seo Daeryong had not even bothered with a simple disguise. If they had come wearing so much as a fake beard, they might have avoided suspicion, but they had deliberately done no such thing. This was all part of Goweol’s operation.

Jisaeng asked Geom Mugeuk,

“When did you become one of the Iron Sword Four Tigers?”

Geom Mugeuk glanced at Daeho, then spoke in a trembling voice.

“It’s been quite some time.”

His vague answer, deliberately avoiding an exact time, caused the corners of Jisaeng’s mouth to rise.

‘The rumor may be true.’

He assumed that the brothers had died while raiding the caravan, and that Daeho had recruited new men afterward.

As if he had nothing to hide, Daeho said boldly,

“Some of my younger brothers were recently injured or left, so I took in new ones. They’re all trustworthy men, so you needn’t worry about Sinseonchae.”

Concealing his vicious thoughts, Jisaeng spoke in a pleasant tone.

“Seeing these young and stalwart tigers gives me confidence. Devote yourselves to the greater cause. I’ll repay you with wealth, honor, and glory.”

“We will give you our utmost loyalty.”

Thus ended the first meeting between Jisaeng and the Iron Sword Four Tigers.

* * *

Three days later, in a speeding carriage, Jisaeng sent a voice transmission to the real Jisaeng.

―The man named Daeho among the Iron Sword Four Tigers possesses a top-grade Night Pearl.

The real Jisaeng brought the carriage to a halt. No matter how important the matter, he had always exchanged voice transmissions while driving the carriage. But this time, he stopped it altogether.

―Is that true?

―Yes.

The real Jisaeng could not conceal his excitement. Top-grade Night Pearls were his favorite treasures, and he collected them by any means necessary.

Among treasures, there were two whose prices only rose and never fell. They were Night Pearls and Poison-Repelling Pearls.

Jisaeng bought Night Pearls whenever money accumulated. He did not buy cheap ones. He sought only the finest Night Pearls.

He did not put his money in the gambling houses. Yulhan or the Evil Path Alliance might one day search his gambling house and take all the money he had amassed. They possessed that much power, and were more than capable of doing so.

So he kept only a portion of his money in the gambling houses and converted the rest entirely into Night Pearls. The reason he chose Night Pearls rather than gold, paintings, or works of art was that they were small and light, easy to store and transport.

―Could someone be trying to trick us?

In response to the suspicious question, the false Jisaeng answered confidently,

―That’s why I checked again through an information broker. It’s certain. They raided the caravan, and three of the Iron Sword Four Tigers died in the process.

He did not know that Goweol had spread the rumor—and that Goweol controlled the information in this region.

―There is one strange thing, though. They made such a large sum of money, yet they still accepted this assignment.

―Have you ever seen greed be satisfied?

That was what Jisaeng felt every time he looked at the real Jisaeng.

―Perhaps he was trying to hide beneath the elder’s shadow.

―How many does he have?

―They say three.

Not one, but three. There was no need for even a moment’s hesitation.

―Get rid of him as quickly as possible and recover the Night Pearls. Find others to entrust with Sinseonchae.

―The Central Plains are full of scum like the Iron Sword Four Tigers. I’ll find someone soon.

No one could restrain him when it came to greed for wealth.

―I went hunting for venison and ended up with tiger hide!

Jisaeng laughed heartily and set the carriage moving again.

There was only one thing that could make him laugh so boisterously.

* * *

The Iron Sword Four Tigers received an invitation from Jisaeng.

When they arrived, food and alcohol had been prepared in the room. The side dishes were meager, and the liquor was such cheap swill that it was difficult even to put it to one’s lips.

Guessing what this spread signified, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“We’re all one family now. Would you mind removing the screen between us? I’d like to see the elder’s esteemed countenance.”

Jisaeng laughed from behind the screen.

“Young men truly are full of vigor. Very well.”

The screen between Jisaeng and the Iron Sword Four Tigers was removed. Since Jisaeng had already decided to kill them all, he had no reason to hide his face.

Geom Mugeuk raised his cup.

“Come, let’s have a drink together!”

Jisaeng did not raise his cup. He revealed his cold emotions, and the atmosphere naturally froze.

“Daeho, I heard a rumor about you.”

“What rumor are you referring to?”

“I heard you came into possession of something valuable.”

“I’m afraid I don’t know what you mean.”

“We’re all family here. Let’s have a look.”

“I truly don’t know what you mean.”

Daeho flatly denied it.

Jisaeng nodded.

“Yes, of course you would. I would have done the same. I understand.”

As soon as those words fell, four martial artists entered the room. They were filled with killing intent and possessed martial skills superior to those of the Iron Sword Four Tigers.

They were the limbs Jisaeng cherished most. He mainly used them when he needed to kill someone discreetly.

“Since those three newcomers don’t know anything, kill them first.”

Geom Mugeuk then said to Daeho,

“Poor Big Brother. He’s about to lose all his younger brothers again.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Daeho sighed and looked at Jisaeng. His eyes seemed to say,

‘You’re in deep trouble now, too.’

An inexplicable sense of disharmony sent a chill through Jisaeng’s chest. He shouted at his subordinates,

“Kill them at once!”

The instant the words left his mouth, the four men collapsed all at once, crumpling to the floor.

Jisaeng was so shocked that his face turned pale. All four of his subordinates had died without even getting the chance to swing their swords. He had ordered them to kill—not to die—so why had they all—

“Poison!”

The Poison King was already waving his fan gently back and forth. It was his unique weapon, the Endless Fan.

Even though all four men had been poisoned simultaneously, the others in the room remained unharmed. This astonishing technique was a feat of poison arts that only the Poison King could perform. When he wielded poison, there was no trace of cuteness or youthful innocence about him. Cold and ruthless, he seemed like an entirely different person.

Jisaeng sprang to his feet and drew his sword.

Just as he was about to shout and alert the real Jisaeng and the other subordinates waiting outside, Geom Mugeuk raised his index finger to his lips.

Shh!

It was only a gesture telling him to be quiet, yet Jisaeng’s mute acupoint had already been sealed.

“You invited us here and served us liquor this cheap. This will probably be better.”

Staring at the small vial of poison held out toward him, Jisaeng realized that the greatest crisis of his life had come to the life he had enjoyed so gleefully under the protection of the Evil Path Alliance.

And it was not merely a crisis.

It was a crisis of unimaginable proportions.

Chapter 219: To Avoid This Fate.
--------------------------------
Watching Jisaeng force the poison down, Daeho felt inwardly relieved.

One more person had ended up in the same situation as him.

‘They’re treating a subordinate of Yelü Han, the second-in-command of the Sado Alliance, like this?’

It was truly a sight hard to believe even as he watched it.

Naturally, another question arose. Just who were these people?

Geom Mugeuk said to Jisaeng,

“If you don’t take the antidote every day, your internal organs will melt and you’ll die. You’ll probably suffer the greatest pain a human can experience. So if you want to scream, go ahead.”

Geom Mugeuk released Jisaeng’s mute acupoint. Jisaeng’s mouth, clamped shut like a clam, did not open.

Jisaeng was so shocked and bewildered that his mind seemed to go blank. He, too, had only the same thought as Daeho.

‘Just who are these people, to do this to me?’

The next instant, one thought flashed through his mind.

‘Ah! Could this be a test of loyalty?’

Once he thought of it that way, it seemed possible. They would tell him to spill the Alliance’s secrets, torture him, and test his loyalty.

But the next moment, his gaze turned to his four subordinates lying cold and dead.

‘They would sacrifice them just to test me?’

There was no way. Then why on earth?

Just then, Geom Mugeuk approached and held out a box he had not expected.

“Open it.”

When Jisaeng opened the box, inside were three top-quality night pearls. They were the very night pearls Geom Mugeuk had once received after saving the Golden Manor Lord, his daughter, and his grandson.

Jisaeng could not understand what situation this was at all.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Take them to him. To the real Jisaeng. We know your coachman is the real Jisaeng.”

The fake Jisaeng was so shocked he could not say a word.

“You were going to kill us and take these, weren’t you? No?”

That was true, but he had never imagined he would receive them like this.

“Either simply take these to him as we tell you, or give them to him and reveal everything that happened just now. The choice is yours.”

“Why are you doing this?”

Why were they giving him a choice?

“Know this before you choose. The poison you took has no antidote to be found anywhere. Only that esteemed person over there possesses the antidote.”

The Poison King was sitting where he had been, merely fanning himself lightly. He paid no attention to what was happening here.

“Ah, one more thing. Think about how your master will react if you reveal the truth. Will he really risk danger and come find us to negotiate in order to save you? He’ll think he has to return these three night pearls, of course, and hand over other things as well.”

Jisaeng’s face remained stiff throughout the explanation. He knew best what choice the real Jisaeng would make.

“Die after offering your loyalty, or live after handing over the night pearls. I’m not telling you to kill him. I’m just telling you to give him the night pearls.”

“You aren’t trying to harm the elder?”

“Wouldn’t that be even better for you? The higher-ups would probably seat you in that position in Jisaeng’s place.”

“!”

It would be a lie to say he had never once imagined such a situation before. Perhaps because this was an opportunity that had come in the worst possible circumstances, those words sounded all the more tempting.

“Think it over on the way back.”

Geom Mugeuk did not give him time.

‘They’re going to let me live? They’re giving the night pearls to the elder? Why on earth?’

In his current confusion, his mind was not working properly.

As if driven out, Jisaeng left the building and walked toward the carriage in the yard. The real Jisaeng was seated on the coachman’s seat as always, blankly looking up at the night sky.

The fake Jisaeng climbed into the carriage.

“Let’s go!”

The carriage set off. The real Jisaeng paid no mind to the fact that the direct subordinates who usually followed him were nowhere to be seen. He assumed they had stayed behind to dispose of the corpses of the Four Iron Sword Tigers.

The real Jisaeng’s voice transmission came.

—How did it go?

—It was resolved well.

Jisaeng handed the box through the small window leading to the coachman’s seat.

Jisaeng knew. The real Jisaeng was absolutely not the sort of man who would suffer financial loss in order to save him. Even if he worked under him for a hundred years, that truth would not change.

Jisaeng on the coachman’s seat opened the box containing the night pearls and broke into a radiant smile. His two eyes looked as if they might be sucked into the night pearls.

Even at that very moment, the fake Jisaeng was agonizing.

‘What exactly are they after?’

They were just giving away the night pearls like this? Why on earth?

But the moment he uttered the words that it had been resolved well, this matter became irreversible.

Even if he revealed the truth belatedly, he would be killed for having harbored the thought of betrayal, even if only for a brief instant.

And so, carrying the opposing hearts of the two Jisaengs, the carriage returned to their residence.

* * *

There are moments when Jisaeng returns from being the coachman to being the real Jisaeng.

When he meets Yelü Han, and right now.

A secret space prepared beneath the stable.

In a space of a little over ten pyeong lay everything that belonged to him.

There was a small chair in the center of the room, and in display cabinets on all sides were several dozen supreme-quality night pearls.

They were the fortune he had gathered over his lifetime.

Jisaeng added the three night pearls to his collection. His hands were trembling. Having gathered so much wealth, he should have become indifferent by now, but he loved this moment too much.

Each time a night pearl was added, he felt the rapture of being alive. Nothing else gave him this much joy. Especially when he put out the lanterns and looked at the night pearls shining in the darkness, he would sometimes let out mad, monstrous cries.

For this joy, he had lived a merciless life. Because he had to offer money to Yelü Han, he had been forced to become even more vicious and cruel. Pangs of conscience? He did not think he had ever felt them once. Whenever someone spoke of guilt, this was the first thought that came to mind. Who told you to live like such an idiot? If you feel wronged, then live like this too.

Just as he was sinking into ecstasy again today, Jisaeng sensed a presence behind him.

Startled, he spun around with lightning speed, and someone was standing before the door. It was the fake Jisaeng.

Only now did the fake Jisaeng finally understand.

Why they had given the night pearls to the real Jisaeng. They had been aiming for the moment those night pearls were brought here. In order to discover where this secret vault was. But how on earth had they known he collected night pearls?

The real Jisaeng, for his part, was terribly shocked.

This secret space beneath an old, shabby stable was a place no one could have imagined. Who in the world would guess that treasure was hidden in a place like this?

Moreover, in order to enter this place, one had to correctly operate, in succession, the secret mechanisms hidden throughout the stable before the door would open.

Of course, Jisaeng had checked and checked his surroundings several times before entering. There had definitely been no one. With that fake Jisaeng’s skill, he should not have been able to approach so closely. Unless someone had night vision as keen as a beast’s, even if they saw him, they would not have known what he was doing.

‘Damn it! Damn it all!’

A wave of self-reproach swept over him, telling him that because nothing had happened for so many years, he had unknowingly let down his guard.

In any case, one thing was certain.

Betrayal!

If he had been coveting Jisaeng’s wealth and preparing for a long time, perhaps he could have infiltrated this secret place.

Instead of asking how he had entered this place, or why he had come in—

Swish!

A dagger flew in a surprise attack from Jisaeng’s hand. He was a master of throwing knives. Since he did not have to carry a sword or saber, it had been easier for him to pose as a coachman.

The dagger flying toward the fake Jisaeng’s throat stopped in midair.

Someone had reached out from behind him and caught the incoming dagger. It was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

Rage surged up in the fake Jisaeng.

He had devoted more than ten years of loyalty to him like a dog. Yet he tried to kill him without even asking a single question. At the very least, he should have asked why he had betrayed him before killing him.

Behind the fake Jisaeng, Geom Mugeuk revealed himself, and after him, Seo Daeryong and Daeho appeared.

“You damned bastards!”

Seeing Daeho, Jisaeng mistook the people who had appeared for the Four Iron Sword Tigers. In this situation, he had no choice but to think so. That this fake Jisaeng and the Four Iron Sword Tigers had conspired to steal his wealth.

Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!

Daggers flew from his hand once more.

But every dagger he had boasted never missed was caught in Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

Jisaeng was horrified. That had not been an attack the likes of the Four Iron Sword Tigers could block.

Geom Mugeuk did not give him a chance to throw the next dagger. Closing in with the Nether King Step, he sealed Jisaeng’s paralysis acupoint. In a flash, his body stiffened, and Jisaeng was seized by fear as he saw Geom Mugeuk standing before his eyes.

“You’re not the Four Iron Sword Tigers!”

“Of course we aren’t. How could the likes of the Four Iron Sword Tigers deal with you?”

Geom Mugeuk passed him and looked over the display cabinet behind him.

“You collected quite a lot.”

“Who are you? Who the hell are you!”

When Geom Mugeuk did not answer, the sparks of his rage flew toward the fake Jisaeng.

“You useless idiot! You absolute moron!”

The fake Jisaeng shouted back.

“I was loyal to you like a dog for fifteen years! And yet you threw a dagger without even asking why? You should have asked first. Asked whether I was threatened and dragged here, you… you dog of a bastard!”

Their relationship, which had seemed so good in ordinary times, shattered in an instant. The dignity and composure befitting an elder vanished as well.

“Because you must not be in this place!”

“Why not? This is filthy wealth gathered by taking, deceiving, and killing. Why can’t I come to a place like this?”

“Was I the one who did those things? You committed them all.”

“You call that an argument?”

The two raised the veins in their necks as they shouted.

“You pathetic fool. Do you think they’ll let you live?”

“I’m going to take your place.”

“Do you think the main Alliance is that naive? They won’t leave you alive, but even if you survive, the main Alliance won’t let you live. With me dead, why would they go out of their way to leave you, an unpleasant loose end, to keep handling the work? They can just clean everything out and set up a new board. Like how we created Sinseonchae anew.”

The fake Jisaeng could not retort. He had dragged out the fear the fake Jisaeng had buried deep in his heart, pretending not to know it was there.

“So in the end, I’m being devoured. I was so careful to avoid ending up like this.”

At Jisaeng’s lament, Geom Mugeuk now stepped forward.

“If you were truly anxious, you shouldn’t have increased your caution. You should have reduced your greed.”

Geom Mugeuk sent out finger winds in succession. Now the fake Jisaeng and Daeho had both had their paralysis and mute acupoints sealed. It meant that from now on, everyone was to shut up and listen.

“Today, this is what will happen here. You hired the Four Iron Sword Tigers for Sinseonchae. But those bastards betrayed you. From the beginning, they were after your wealth.”

Geom Mugeuk looked back and forth between Jisaeng and Daeho.

“Of course, there’s a difference in martial skill between you. But that problem can be solved with this.”

Geom Mugeuk opened a small bottle right in front of Jisaeng’s nose.

Sssssss.

Smoke from the bottle was forced into Jisaeng’s nose.

“You were struck by Paralysis Powder. You know it, right? It’s what you unorthodox faction people mainly use. It’s also a poison that remains in the body if an autopsy is done later. Poisoned, you stagger and collapse.”

Geom Mugeuk moved him, whose paralysis acupoint had been sealed, as though moving a puppet.

“At this moment, Daeho stabs you in the back with a sword.”

Seo Daeryong likewise moved Daeho’s body. After placing a sword in Daeho’s hand, he moved his body. Daeho’s sword stabbed into Jisaeng’s back.

Jisaeng’s face twisted in pain, but because his mute acupoint had been sealed, he could not even scream.

“You were poisoned and stabbed with a sword, but you’re not the sort to simply suffer it. You throw a dagger and counterattack.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk took a dagger from Jisaeng’s robe and threw it.

The dagger lodged precisely in Daeho’s heart. He died on the spot and collapsed.

“You succeed in killing Daeho, but the wound from being stabbed in the back is fatal. In the end, you collapse here and meet your death. While watching someone steal away the fortune you spent your whole life gathering.”

In reality, Seo Daeryong began putting every night pearl there into the sack he had brought.

“That must be something you hate even more than dying, right?”

Jisaeng’s eyes were bloodshot red. He tried hard to shake his head, as if begging them not to.

“Everyone had things taken from them by you in exactly this way.”

This would not resolve the resentment of the many young people who had suffered because of Sinseonchae, but he intended to inflict the greatest anguish he could.

“Yayulhan will send men because of your death. In the end, the culprits will be revealed as the Four Iron Sword Tigers. There will be testimony from your subordinates that you tried to hire them, and Daeho’s corpse will be left here.”

Jisaeng looked as though he had a great deal he wanted to say, but Geom Mugeuk did not release his mute acupoint.

“We’ll be taking everything you spent your life gathering. We’ll put it to good use in your place! Thanks!”

Jisaeng’s entire body trembled. As he died, he was filled with nothing but rage and resentment. Geom Mugeuk wanted him to die that way.

Now Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to the fake Jisaeng. He was staring at Geom Mugeuk with eyes that swore he would never reveal what had happened today.

His face was full of hope that he might live, and expectation that he might even become the real Jisaeng.

But Geom Mugeuk shattered that expectation.

“However, there is one problem with this plan. The Sado Alliance won’t believe Jisaeng was done in by the likes of the Four Iron Sword Tigers. So we need a plausible mastermind behind it. Someone who knew his true identity, who could poison him with Paralysis Powder, and who could find out about a secret warehouse. What do you think? You, his right-hand man of fifteen years?”

Bewilderment and anger bloomed across the fake Jisaeng’s face. But the words that followed drove him into even deeper despair.

“Yes, that’s right. The one who ran off with these night pearls will be you. And they will never find you.”

Geom Mugeuk’s fist slammed into his chest, and with that single blow, his insides were shattered and he died on the spot. From the beginning, Geom Mugeuk had never intended to spare a man who had committed every manner of evil. He would be buried somewhere he could never be found.

The dying Jisaeng smiled as he looked at the fake Jisaeng’s corpse, as though it served him right. The sight of him laughing while blood flowed from his mouth was grotesque. But he was certainly not fated to die laughing.

Just then, Seo Daeryong, who had recovered all the night pearls and was standing to one side, let out an affected sigh and said,

“Is this always how a right-hand man meets his end?”

“My right-hand man is smart, so I don’t think he’d fall for something like this.”

“If you keep saying things that make me feel this good, how am I supposed to quit being your perilous right-hand man?”

“That’s how everyone falls for it and ends up like this.”

After joking like that, Seo Daeryong approached the dying Jisaeng.

“But why did you keep looking over there? When you were busy enough dying as it was?”

In that instant, Jisaeng grew flustered. Blood suddenly gushed from his mouth.

“Now this really looks suspicious.”

If he had simply glanced, they would have thought nothing of it. But Jisaeng had looked at the wall with eyes full of regret. And that happened to catch Seo Daeryong’s eye.

Geom Mugeuk and Seo Daeryong gave each other a nod, then began examining the wall.

As Geom Mugeuk slowly inspected it, the moment his fingertips caught on something, a crisp metallic click rang out.

In that instant, Jisaeng despaired as though the heavens had collapsed. It was an even greater despair than when the night pearls had been taken from him earlier.

With his mute acupoint sealed, no words came out, but the shape of his mouth was saying this:

‘Not that. Anything but that.’

Bzzzzzz.

The floor in front of the wall opened, and something began to rise.

Chapter 220: You Should Be Yourself.
------------------------------------
What rose from the floor was a dais.

On top of it sat a flat box covered in green cloth.

Seodaeryong and I both cried out at the sight of the dazzling object inside the box.

It was a night-luminous pearl. But it was different from all the night-luminous pearls we had collected so far. It was larger than even a supreme-grade night-luminous pearl, and its radiance was even more splendid.

“It’s the Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl!”

This was the finest night-luminous pearl, known as the king of all night-luminous pearls.

Upon seeing the object that had existed only in rumors, Seodaeryong opened his eyes wide in admiration.

“So it really did exist!”

Just then, a groan rose from the floor. Jisaeng’s eyes were bloodshot. He was on the verge of going mad from the shock. Even if he combined the value of every other night-luminous pearl, he could not buy this one. Not because it shone so brightly, but because there was only one in existence.

I calmly watched Jisaeng suffer.

His pain was still insufficient. He was paying for his crimes, but the people who had suffered at his hands had been struck by calamity in the midst of peaceful lives. If he had made hundreds, even thousands, of lives miserable, then he deserved to suffer at least this much.

For him, dying would have been a blessing, but even the phenomenon of a final rally had come upon him. He would have to endure the last pain of his life with a clear mind.

I carefully picked up the Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl.

“Right now, you must be holding the most expensive object in the world, measured by price. Please let me touch it just once. Ah, no. I might drop it. I’ll just look at it.”

At that moment, as I stared at its enchanting light, I tightened my grip.

Crack.

A long fracture split across the Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl.

It was enough to bring the dying Jisaeng springing to his feet. Jisaeng’s eyes flew open as though they might tear apart, while Seodaeryong screamed in his stead.

“Uaaaaaaagh!”

He would not have screamed like that even after being stabbed by a sword.

“You don’t have to touch it! Why are you doing this?”

I applied a little more force.

Craaaaaack.

The Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl began to crumble in my hand.

“Uaaaaaaagh!”

Seodaeryong screamed again. If Seodaeryong was reacting like this, what about Jisaeng? Tears were streaming from Jisaeng’s eyes.

Seodaeryong spoke in a trembling voice.

“I understand that you wish to inflict pain on that man, but how could you break an Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl?”

His voice trembled. His expression seemed to say, This really isn’t right.

“Take off your clothes!”

“You really do look insane when you act like this!”

Though he said that, Seodaeryong hurriedly took off his upper garment.

“Spread it out in front of me!”

Rattle-rattle-rattle.

Fragments of the night-luminous pearl fell onto Seodaeryong’s clothes.

I searched among the fragments for something.

There it is!

I picked up the object lying among the shards.

Black and lustrous like a black pearl, it was a single pill.

The Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill.

The Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill was the supreme elixir among poisons, created by blending every kind of deadly toxin.

“This Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl is a fake. It was made to conceal the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill.”

Before my regression, I had heard that the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill had been discovered inside an Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl. It had been found by chance when a fierce struggle for the pearl broke out among demonic-path martial artists.

I knew who possessed the real Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl, but even I had not known that this fake one had been kept in Jisaeng’s possession.

“It’s the safest way to hide something in the world. No one would have the courage to break this.”

Both Seodaeryong and Jisaeng looked utterly shocked. It must have been something they had never imagined.

“How did you know?”

I answered Seodaeryong’s astonished question as I always did.

“What is there that I do not know?”

As always, Seodaeryong shook his head and asked no further questions.

“If it were another elixir, I would share it with you, but no one except me can consume this. Only someone immune to all poisons can take it.”

The only person who knew that I was immune to all poisons was Seodaeryong.

“Even if I could take it, you should consume it, Sect Leader.”

Seodaeryong’s gaze turned toward the corpse of the fake Jisaeng.

“If I were threatened like that man, I would run straight to you and tell you everything. I’d beg you to save me. I’d tell you to take responsibility. To save me then, Sect Leader, you would have to become stronger.”

Even if it had been an ordinary elixir, Seodaeryong would probably have yielded it to me with words like those. They say I’m the kind of person who gives one and receives ten in return? What about you?

“You would never have obtained this elixir if not for you. Thank you, Investigator Seo.”

If I had not noticed Jisaeng gazing at the wall with regret in his eyes, this would have remained buried underground forever.

“Name that elixir the Right Arm’s Keen Eye.”

“I’ll add ‘with an especially keen eye’ to the description introducing you.”

“My introduction has become so long that I can’t memorize it anymore.”

Seodaeryong laughed happily. How could he dislike someone attaching so much meaning to who he was? In our lives, even gaining a single meaning was difficult.

“I’ll stand guard, so consume it at once, Sect Leader.”

I sat down cross-legged where I stood.

At the sight of this, Jisaeng let out another groan. He was suffering genuinely. He was enduring pain greater than having his flesh seared with heated iron, yet the heavens would not let him go easily.

I swallowed the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill.

I considered it fate that I had become immune to all poisons and obtained this miraculous poison pill here.

A force hotter and more intense than any elixir I had ever consumed before flowed down my throat. Its medicinal energy was so fierce that it felt as though my internal organs were burning away.

I regulated my body through circulating qi and began dissolving the energy of the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill. The energy raced through my blood vessels, spreading into every corner of my body, even the smallest meridians. It was an energy I could never have endured without the Meridian Tempering Art, which I had completed to its final stage, and my Poison-Immune Physique.

After dissolving all the energy of the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill and circulating my true qi through one full cycle, an incomparably vigorous and pure internal force was added to my dantian.

The fullness I felt in my dantian was different from when I had consumed other elixirs. It was not merely the feeling of, Ah, I have a great deal of internal force now. Rather, it felt as though I had climbed one step higher and reached the internal force of the next realm.

It was as though my internal force itself were saying this:

I am now ready to face a new world.

“Congratulations, Sect Leader.”

“I’ll say it again, but this is all thanks to you. Thank you, Right Arm.”

I felt wonderful. I had to become stronger. Only by becoming stronger could I protect everyone.

With my gaze clearer and deeper than before, I neatly folded the green cloth that had wrapped the Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl and tucked it into my breast. As would be revealed later, this too was an extremely important artifact.

Then Jisaeng, whom I had thought dead, suddenly opened his eyes. He had clung stubbornly to life, and he pleaded with desperate eyes.

He begged me to release his mute acupoint just once.

There were too many things he wanted to know, and too many things he wanted to say. But I was resolute.

“Have you ever once granted the requests of those who begged you?”

Regret and resentment rose to their peak, and then the light vanished from Jisaeng’s eyes. His lips moved one last time.

It’s all mine.

With those words, he died.

He might have realized the futility of greed and put his life in order during his final moments, but he never abandoned his greed, not even at the end. Even as he died, he shouldered all the night-luminous pearls and chewed down the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill before boarding the ferry to the underworld.

This is why. The orthodox path can never endure such vicious longing and the malice contained within it. Someone will be deceived by that desperate gaze and give him another chance. Because he pleads so earnestly, because he is facing death, because he is still human.
