Evil does not grow on its own. It grows by devouring the goodwill of others. An absolute evil being is one who, even while dying, plants the seeds of malice in the heart of someone who showed him kindness before he departs this world.

In any case, all the villains connected to Fresh Immortal Village had now met their deaths.

“I don’t think I could do what you did, Sect Leader. I don’t know whether I could punish villains so decisively.”

“You shouldn’t act like me.”

“What do you mean?”

I stared straight into his eyes and said,

“You should act like yourself.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Seodaeryong’s mouth.

“Yes. I’ll act like myself.”

Seodaeryong gathered the sack filled with night-luminous pearls, while I tucked the fake Jisaeng’s corpse under my arm.

Looking down at the real Jisaeng’s corpse, I said,

“Your death is only the beginning.”

Everything disappeared.

The villains’ lives, their greed, their shallow faith, their betrayals, their lingering attachments, the fortunes they had amassed over lifetimes—and even Seodaeryong and me. Everything that had been tangled together disappeared.

* * *

“With the Second Young Master being so outstanding, it seems we won’t be needed.”

At the Poison King’s words, the Extreme Evil Little Demon glanced back.

At first, speaking even a single word had been so difficult for the two of them, but once the conversation began, they started speaking with surprising ease.

“Wouldn’t you agree?”

Geom Mugeuk had taken only Seodaeryong with him, saying that he would handle the finishing work himself.

“Did you want to follow them?”

“Not necessarily.”

In truth, the Poison King had wanted to follow. He wanted to see how Geom Mugeuk handled things, but when the two left alone, he felt oddly disappointed.

“There will be plenty of things we need to step in for from now on. Yaryulhan in particular won’t be easy for the Second Young Master to face alone.”

Worry crossed the Extreme Evil Little Demon’s eyes. The Poison King also nodded in agreement. Even if he did not go outside, as a Demon Venerable, he still heard and saw many things.

“Yaryulhan could be called the most dangerous deadly poison among deadly poisons.”

The Extreme Evil Little Demon’s concern did not end with Yaryulhan.

“I’ve heard that, apart from the Four Great Guardians, there are absolute experts separately protecting Yaryulhan. Yaryulhan himself is strong, but those experts will not be people we can ignore either. When the time comes, we may have to risk our lives as well.”

Their opponent was the second-in-command of the Demonic Alliance. He would certainly have hidden blades, but how sharp they were—and how many there were—remained unknown.

The Poison King asked the Extreme Evil Little Demon,

“Can you really risk your life for the Second Young Master?”

The Extreme Evil Little Demon paused before answering.

“I enjoy fighting alongside the Second Young Master.”

The Poison King heard those words as meaning that he could risk his life for the Second Young Master.

The Poison King was envious. He had no one. No one whose life he could risk, and no one who would risk their life for him.

That was why these words came out for no particular reason.

“I only came out because I was ordered to by the Cult Leader, so I had no choice…”

The Extreme Evil Little Demon silently stared at the Poison King. His eyes asked: Is that really so?

The Poison King was about to say that it was, but then he fell into thought. Perhaps it was because of the Second Young Master’s mourning, the Second Young Master’s efforts, or the fact that he enjoyed meeting the Second Young Master.

Perhaps another reason had influenced him as well. He could not answer in the end.

“It’s hot today.”

For no reason, the Poison King opened his innocent fan and began waving it gently.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Seodaeryong returned to their quarters.

“I’ve wrapped things up nicely.”

Geom Mugeuk told the Demon Venerables everything that had happened there and everything they had obtained. There was no need to tell them, but he honestly revealed every detail without leaving anything out.

There was only one exception: he omitted the matter of the Ultimate Night-Luminous Pearl and the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill. Explaining how he had known that the pill was inside the pearl would have been a problem, and it was not yet time to reveal his immunity to all poisons.

“We’ll leave early tomorrow morning.”

At my words, the Poison King asked,

“Who is the next target?”

“Aech'a, the man with the black snake tattoo. He is also the leader of the True Poison Hermit, whom you have been eager to meet.”

“I’ll finally meet that third-rate poison peddler.”

It was an insult only the Poison King could make. The True Poison Hermit was not an opponent to be taken lightly. He was a poison arts master who represented the demonic path.

Aech'a had joined hands with the True Poison Hermit and was selling drugs such as Gwangpok and Heukbi.

“Have you ever met the True Poison Hermit, Poison King?”

“No.”

The Poison King shook his head at my question.

“That’s fortunate. It would be difficult to approach him secretly if you knew his face. This time, I’ll need your help, Poison King.”

The Poison King nodded. He had decided to help enthusiastically in this matter, at least. He truly disliked the True Poison Hermit, who manufactured and sold drugs that caused side effects. Leaving everything else aside, this was a matter of a poison master’s pride.

“According to Military Advisor Go’s information, the True Poison Hermit is producing Gwangpok and Heukbi in the deepest part of Aech'a’s residence. Naturally, the place is guarded so thoroughly that infiltrating it is practically impossible.”

At that moment, Seodaeryong cautiously asked,

“But setting Gwangpok aside, is it all right to eliminate Aech'a as well? With Jisaeng dead, if Aech'a dies immediately afterward, Yaryulhan will become suspicious.”

“We must give him a death that cannot arouse suspicion. We’ll make him believe that the coincidences simply overlapped.”

“How?”

Everyone’s eyes turned toward me. I had been thinking of one method. I did not know whether it would work.

“Since this all began with Gwangpok, I intend to end it with Gwangpok.”

Chapter 221: Anyone Want to Say Who They Are?
---------------------------------------------
That evening.

I trained in martial arts deep in the mountains, away from our quarters. Even after leaving the cult, I devoted myself to training whenever I had the chance.

At first, watching me, the Poison King looked doubtful, as if suspecting it was merely a performance. But soon, seeing that I did not waste even the smallest scraps of time, he shook his head and called me tenacious. But how could the training I did be ordinary training?

Martial arts change only when the person changes.

This was one of the ultimate principles of martial learning as I understood it, but there was one condition. Even while striving to change oneself, one had to train in martial arts as if one were dying. If the will ran ahead but the training did not follow, then even if the person changed, the martial art would not.

First I trained in the Thunderbolt Asura Fist, then practiced the Wind God Four Steps, and I did not neglect the Flying Heaven Sword Art either.

At the end, I trained by mixing all three martial arts together. If I were to face a truly powerful enemy, the key to victory or defeat would be how efficiently I could bring these three martial arts to bear.

As my inner force increased through the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill, its quality also changed. It felt deeper and purer. As a result, even when I used the same amount of inner force to execute a technique, its power had grown stronger.

Finally, standing atop a high boulder and gazing at the setting sun, I brought my training to an end.

* * *

After finishing a bout of training, Geom Mugeuk stopped by the marketplace.

The place he entered was the home of an old woman said to have the finest hands in the town.

“Is it finished?”

“Here it is. What do you think?”

Geom Mugeuk checked what the old woman held out and smiled.

“It is excellent.”

After paying the promised price, Geom Mugeuk left the place.

When he returned to their quarters, the place he headed for was the Poison King’s room.

The Poison King was pacing alone in his room, lost in thought. Then, as if something had occurred to him, he rushed to the desk and picked up a brush.

He wrote diligently whatever had come to mind, then seemed to hit another block and rose from his seat to pace the room. Then he sank into deep thought again. Then he suddenly sprang forward and wrote something down.

“You still haven’t gone to sleep?”

When Geom Mugeuk entered, the Poison King closed the booklet he had been writing in.

“It’s late. What brings you here?”

“I came because I have something for you.”

Geom Mugeuk held out the item he had had made in the marketplace.

It was a pair of gloves.

“What is this?”

At a glance, the gloves gave off an unusual air.

“They are gloves made from Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk.”

The Poison King was startled.

“Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk?”

Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk was a fabric incomparably more effective than ordinary green-hide gloves that warded off poison. If Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk was resistant to external impact, this Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk was an item resistant to poison.

“Where did you find something this precious?”

“I found it in Jisaeng’s secret storehouse. I entrusted it to the most skillful old woman in the market and had it made into gloves. Try them on. I had them made the same size as the gloves you wore in the Thousand Poison Forest, so they should fit well.”

The green cloth that had wrapped the fake supreme-grade luminous pearl had been Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk. It had been used to wrap the pearl in order to protect the aura of the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill within it. The deceased person who had hidden the Myriad Poisons Ultimate Divine Pill inside the luminous pearl had undoubtedly been someone who had founded an entire lineage through poison.

“Why are you giving me something this precious?”

“Because it is precious. If anything, it’s stranger that you, Poison King, have never had something like this until now.”

“Have you ever seen a master calligrapher fuss over his brush?”

“A brush doesn’t kill people. From now on, when you handle deadly poisons, please be sure to use these.”

And he also gave him the remaining cloth.

“I brought the rest separately, thinking you might have some use for it.”

After handing over the remaining Poison-Repelling Heavenly Silkworm Silk without hesitation, Geom Mugeuk turned away.

“Then please get some sleep. We will leave early tomorrow.”

Geom Mugeuk bid him farewell and left the room.

It had happened so suddenly that the Poison King had not even been able to thank him properly.

He stared blankly down at the gloves and cloth. Then something caught his eye. Words had been embroidered onto the gloves.

On the back of one glove:

Above Heaven and Below Heaven.

On the other glove:

I Alone Am the Poison Sovereign.

It was the phrase “I Alone Am Supreme,” but the original character for “alone” had been changed to the character for “poison.”

Emotion crossed the Poison King’s face.

Becoming the Poison Sovereign was his dream. And he had never once revealed that dream to anyone. He had never even told Sangseon, who served him with utmost devotion.

In the end, the Poison King shook his head and said,

“So this is why everyone knew and still fell for it. Truly, it is a poison with no antidote.”

But he could not hide the pleasant emotion that brushed the corners of his eyes.

* * *

The snake tattoo etched on the man’s chest continued all the way to his neck, its long tail coiling once around his throat.

A man with a snake wrapped around his neck.

He was Aecha, one of Yaryulhan’s four retainers. There was a reason he had chosen a snake out of all animals. He had the eyes of a snake. If one looked only at his eyes, it would not be wrong to call him a snake. On top of that, his cold and vicious bearing made him feel even more serpentine.

Reading the letter, Aecha said,

“They say Jisaeng is dead.”

The person startled before him was Jindok Geosa.

“How?”

Jindok Geosa was dressed neatly like a scholar, but his skin was dark and wrinkled, so the scholarly appearance he desired did not come through. His voice, too, was an unpleasant, cracked one.

“As you know, that man kept a fake in front of him for a long time. But they say that fake killed Jisaeng and ran off with his wealth.”

He was delivering the news calmly, but there was no mourning in it. He conveyed only fact, not emotion.

“That man was so cautious that he was not someone who would die easily.”

Jisaeng’s death was truly unexpected. Even if everyone else died, he was not one who seemed likely to die so easily.

But the Alliance’s investigation had concluded with certainty that it was the fake’s doing. Since the one who had died was Jisaeng, they must have investigated thoroughly.

Jindok Geosa, who had been listening quietly, brought up something unexpected.

“Perhaps it was only natural.”

He spoke the words Aecha had wanted to say.

“That man pretended to be a coachman and acted as though he were some great mysterious figure, but from what I saw, he was nothing more than a coward. A coward’s end cannot be good.”

Sure enough, a strange smile formed at the corner of Aecha’s mouth.

“Now that he is dead, all the delayed assessments are coming out.”

It was a mocking rebuke, asking why he was saying such things only now.

In that instant, irritation surged in Jindok Geosa.

‘If I said what you wanted to say in your stead, then even if you do not agree, you should at least not spit on it.’

It was always like this. In situations like this, Aecha always said something offensive.

Jindok Geosa considered it a twisted desire to dominate.

A man who tried to bind others with negative words and stand above them in the relationship. This was how he handled his subordinates.

Hiding his discomfort, Jindok Geosa smiled and said,

“That is why the dead are the only ones to be pitied, is it not?”

Aecha was so cruel by nature that Jindok Geosa had seen many times how those who once fell out of his favor eventually died. But that did not mean he had any intention of being cowed.

Whoosh. Aecha burned the letter he held in the lamplight. Until the very end, there was not a single word of mourning.

When the letter turned to ash and disappeared, Aecha asked abruptly,

“You still haven’t fixed the side effects?”

For an instant, the corner of Jindok Geosa’s eye twitched. It was not easy to keep making a person feel this bad so consistently. This bastard surely lived as though he had three lives.

“I am working on it.”

“You must fix it quickly. Recently, an incident occurred on the Demonic Cult side. If a family member of an important figure from the Demonic Cult or the Murim Alliance is affected, things will become troublesome.”

In truth, what Aecha feared was not the Demonic Cult or the Murim Alliance. It was Yaryulhan’s reprimand in the aftermath. The only person he feared in this world was Yaryulhan.

“I will resolve it as quickly as possible.”

“How long will it take?”

“Give me six months.”

“I will give you one month.”

Jindok Geosa’s expression hardened.

He had expected the period to be shortened and had asked for six months. He had thought Aecha would tell him to finish it within three months, but one month? This was an open act of contempt.

A taut tension flowed between the two men.

Jindok Geosa felt the urge to release poison all over that face. For some reason, Aecha did not fear his poison. Then again, it was precisely because he was such a person that Yaryulhan had assigned him to keep Jindok Geosa under control.

“I will resolve it as quickly as possible.”

“Let us do that.”

For now, Jindok Geosa backed down. But Aecha needed to know this: he was backing down not because he feared him, but because of Yaryulhan.

“Then I will take my leave.”

“I am counting on you, Geosa.”

Aecha glared with snake eyes as Jindok Geosa went outside.

‘All that useless pride.’

If they could not fix Frenzy’s side effects, they would have to abandon Frenzy. But since most of their income came from Frenzy, they absolutely could not give it up.

‘Old man, shouldn’t you first admit you made a mistake?’

Jindok Geosa had not once admitted that he had made it incorrectly.

Aecha, for his part, had things piled up inside him as well.

After coming outside, Jindok Geosa asked the subordinate waiting there,

“How many subjects for the experiments remain?”

“Seven remain.”

“And the next test subjects?”

“They are on their way.”

The Sado Alliance sent warriors to be used in experiments for Jindok Geosa. They were all people who had been imprisoned in the Sado Alliance’s dungeon. They had crossed the line far beyond measure, people even the Sado Alliance had locked away in its dungeon—true scum of the world.

“When are they expected to arrive?”

“It will take about twenty days.”

“Too late! You go and bring them as fast as possible. No matter what it takes, arrive within ten days!”

“Understood.”

The subordinate ran outside.

The subordinate who received the order faithfully carried out the task entrusted to him. Except for one thing: he failed to notice that someone was watching him.

* * *

Ten days later.

“Aaaaargh!”

A man was rampaging in madness. Blood flowed from his eyes and nose.

Watching him, Jindok Geosa let out another sigh.

Of the seven remaining test subjects, even the last one had failed. No matter how he changed the combination of medicinal herbs, he could not prevent the side effects.

The problem was resistance.

At first, one pill had been enough to achieve sufficient effect, but the more one took it, the more resistance built up, requiring larger doses. As the dosage increased, side effects began to appear as well. At present, among hundreds who overdosed, this happened to roughly one person, but if the number of people developing resistance gradually increased, it could become one out of dozens. If that happened, the murim would turn into a battlefield of carnage.

Then a subordinate came and reported,

“The new subjects for the experiments have arrived.”

“How many?”

“Twenty.”

Jindok Geosa went outside with the subordinate.

Two large carriages stood in the yard, and prisoners were getting down from them. The carriages had no windows. They had been transported like cargo. On top of that, they even had black sacks over their heads. Since even their inner force had been suppressed, the stifling discomfort they had suffered must have been beyond description.

Warriors went over and removed the sacks from their heads. The prisoners looked around. The aura of the warriors surrounding them was no ordinary thing, but not one of them showed fear.

And as always, when about twenty lives at rock bottom gathered in one place, there was naturally someone who came forward without fear.

“You bastards! I am Blood Serpent Yang Goeng!”

He was a villain who had once gained infamy in the unorthodox murim.

“No matter how far the code of the martial world has fallen, is this any way to treat a senior?”

He was furious at having been transported like luggage. Moreover, they had covered a distance that should originally have taken twenty days in ten, so the hardship they had endured was beyond words.

Since they were short on people for the experiments, complaints of this degree could have been overlooked.

But he was unlucky. In fact, several pieces of bad luck overlapped.

First, Jindok Geosa was already in a bad mood from having failed in another experiment, and by chance Aecha was watching this scene from the opposite side.

And the final piece of bad luck was that his epithet contained a snake.

Jindok Geosa walked toward him.

“So you are the famous Blood Serpent, senior.”

“Yes, that’s me. And who are you?”

“I am this sort of person.”

Jindok Geosa casually waved his hand.

Just as everyone was watching, wondering what he was doing,

“Aaaaaaargh!”

Blood Serpent suddenly screamed.

Unable to endure the pain, he rolled across the ground. It was not pain a human being could withstand.

Most of those present had committed all manner of evil deeds, yet even they had never seen someone suffer to this extent. They could not even imagine what was happening inside his body.

“Kill me… please!”

The plea to be killed came from Blood Serpent’s mouth.

Jindok Geosa looked down at him coldly.

Blood Serpent begged.

“…Please kill me!”

Jindok Geosa lightly waved his hand again.

Then Blood Serpent’s body began to melt. With a single casual gesture, a person disappeared into bloody liquid. This was the skill of the foremost poison arts master of the unorthodox factions.

No one there dared even breathe loudly.

Jindok Geosa looked toward Aecha in the distance and bowed his head slightly in greeting. His attitude was courteous, but the meaning contained within was clear. Act up, and this will happen to you too.

At that, Aecha gave a deliberate snort of laughter and turned back toward his own quarters.

Watching his back, Jindok Geosa smiled in satisfaction and said,

“Is there anyone else who wishes to say who he is?”

No one dared step forward.

But Jindok Geosa would never know. At that very moment, there was one person who wanted to reveal who he was.

The warriors led the prisoners into the building.

Among those being dragged away in fear were Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King.

Chapter 222: Why Do I Feel Bad When I See You?
----------------------------------------------
The basement had been made up like a dungeon.

The prisoners were each locked in solitary cells. Since Geom Mugeuk had been next to the Poison King, he was assigned the cell across from him.

“You saw what happens to those who cause trouble.”

After intimidating them like that, the martial artists left the place.

The one small mercy was that the iron doors had palm-sized windows through which one could look inside, allowing them to see one another’s faces and talk.

“Damn it! We got out of a dungeon only to be locked up in another dungeon!”

“This place is even filthier than where we were.”

“I once heard the guards talking. They said prisoners get taken out and sent somewhere for secret experiments.”

“Damn it! At this rate, we’ll die like dogs!”

“We have to get out somehow!”

The prisoners each threw in a word, cursing loudly.

Geom Mugeuk sent a sound transmission to the Poison King, whose face had appeared in the window across from him.

-Have you ever been locked in a dungeon before?

-No.

-I’m sorry. For making you suffer through something like this.

-It’s fine. When else would I have such an experience if not at a time like this? Don’t mind me.

The Poison King truly spoke as if it were nothing. In a way, he seemed like the sort of person who would hate being subjected to something like this, but seeing that calm face now, it also seemed as though he would endure it better than any Demon Supreme.

-From what I saw earlier, that third-rate poison hack’s poisoning skill was quite decent.

It was the first time he had praised Jindok Geosa. There must have been something only the Poison King’s eyes could see.

-Whatever else he may be, isn’t he the foremost poison arts master of the unorthodox faction?

-That is why it angers me even more. If he had skill like that, he should have made proper medicine.

About two hours passed like that, and then a meal was brought in.

It was served splendidly, like a feast before death.

Everyone began devouring the food. They had been deliberately starved on the way, and even if they had been full, the food was excellent enough to make their chopsticks move. The reason such delicious food had been provided was simple.

-There’s Berserk in the food.

-They’re feeding us Berserk already?

-Not just Berserk. They’ve put in this drug and that drug too.

-Seeing as they started as soon as we arrived, Yaryul Han or Aecha must have ordered them to hurry things along.

That much was clear just from the fact that they had brought prisoners from twenty-one days away to this place in only ten.

-If anything, this works out well for us. It means we can get out of this stifling place even a day sooner.

-If you take the medicine I gave you beforehand, the Berserk will be neutralized at once.

-Yes.

Of course, even if he did not take it, it could not poison him in the first place. Even the Formless Poison had not worked on him, so there was no way such miscellaneous drugs would.

They ate only the food given to them there, as if they were being fattened like livestock.

Those who had been venting their complaints before the first meal now secretly looked forward to the next. Since superb dishes and even wine, the sort one might find only at a top-class inn, were served, the men who had only eaten garbage-like food in the dungeon went mad with delight as they ate and drank.

-You did make a solid plan, didn’t you?

-Didn’t you say you would leave everything to me?

-It is because you look far too carefree. Training martial arts in here?

Whenever I had time, I sat cross-legged in the middle of the dungeon cell and cultivated the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

Opportunities to remain undisturbed all day like this were rare. The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art had yet to reach great completion, so whenever a chance arose, I focused on cultivating it.

The Poison King seemed curious about me.

-What martial art are you cultivating?

I could not honestly tell him that I had learned the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

-I am training an internal cultivation method.

-Why do you cultivate so diligently?

-Because I believe I must become stronger in order to be free.

-Are you not already strong enough?

-Not enough to enjoy the freedom I want.

-What is the freedom you want?

After a brief pause, I told him.

-A life in which I can stop my steps wherever I wish.

Perhaps it was an unexpected answer, for the Poison King said nothing for a while. It was because he knew well that while it sounded like something anyone could do, it was something no one could easily do.

-The road you walk… is not one where you can stop as you please.

-That is why I am trying. So I can stop when I want and look up at the sky.

There were people close to him with whom he had shared life and death, joy and sorrow, but precisely because of that, he wanted a life where they did not bind one another. That was the freedom Geom Mugeuk desired.

The Poison King silently stared at Geom Mugeuk.

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked.

-Do you have a dream, Poison King?

What dream was the Poison King dreaming now?

-What dream at my age?

Perhaps unwilling to answer, the Poison King disappeared from the small window.

‘Poison King, if you had never met me, that would be one thing. But now that we have met, I will not let you live the fate you once had.’

That was why Geom Mugeuk had engraved the words Poison Sovereign on his gloves.

So that his dream would not become a dark yearning. So that he would think, in a bright place, about what true self-supremacy meant.

Geom Mugeuk sat down and began cultivating the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

* * *

“Please have your meal!”

Seo Daeryong entered Geugak Soma’s room. On the tray in his hands were several neat dishes. Some had been bought, and some he had cooked himself.

Had he ever been this nervous since the day he was born? He probably had not trembled this much even when stepping onto the stage for the final duel of the Little Dragon Tournament.

“Thank you.”

The words were quiet but gentle. His goodwill toward Geom Mugeuk was extending even to Seo Daeryong.

“Please tell me if you need anything!”

Seo Daeryong bowed so low his waist nearly folded in half.

At that moment, Geugak Soma spoke.

“There is no need to be so excessively courteous. You, too, are someone who will become a Demon Supreme one day.”

At the words Demon Supreme, Seo Daeryong’s heart began pounding again. Could he truly become a Demon Supreme on the same level as this terrifying Geugak Soma? Could he really?

He was about to leave, but Seo Daeryong could not control the emotion suddenly welling up inside him and opened his mouth.

“I do not know if I am someone worthy of that.”

At that instant, his reason screamed.

Hey, what are you doing? Shut that mouth!

His head hurriedly tried to stop him, but his mouth, already won over by his heart, was spilling out words.

“I became Master’s disciple by chance, but I truly do not know whether it was because of my own ability or because of the Pavilion Master. I do not know whether this is the right fate for me, or whether I will end up tearing myself apart after strutting around beyond my means. Right now, it feels like I am just being swept along after being pushed from behind. Can someone like me become a Demon Supreme? Can I become someone like you, Soma?”

After finishing, Seo Daeryong felt as if he had been flung up into the air. What had he just done?

‘Ah! I finally caused trouble. Of all the countless people in the world, I had to complain about my lot to Geugak Soma? Asking if I can become Soma? Is that a challenge?’

Seo Daeryong hurriedly bent at the waist.

“I apologize. I must have suddenly lost my mind.”

He was quickly turning around when Geugak Soma spoke.

“The Second Young Master cherishes you greatly.”

Seo Daeryong flinched and turned back to him.

The two eyes inside Geugak Soma’s eye sockets were staring straight at him.

“Yes, I know!”

As soon as he said it, Seo Daeryong realized his mistake.

‘You know? What do you know? You should have said, Thank you for telling me!’

He was so nervous that nonsense kept slipping out.

“No, what I meant just now was…”

Then Geugak Soma said,

“I know as well.”

It sounded as if he were saying that he knew exactly what Seo Daeryong was feeling as he spoke.

Their eyes met. Those eyes were smiling. It was the first time Seo Daeryong had faced Soma’s smile. Seeing those smiling eyes frightened him.

“I made many mistakes today. I am truly sorry.”

Geugak Soma spoke calmly.

“If you made no mistakes before me, would that not be the real mistake?”

Seo Daeryong left the room and let out a sigh. He could not think at all about what had just happened. He had greeted him before leaving, hadn’t he? It truly felt like he had been dreaming. To think he had held such a long conversation alone with Geugak Soma, the most frightening man in the world! He was moved, yet terrified.

‘Pavilion Master! Please come back quickly! Your cherished right arm is going to cause an accident at this rate!’

* * *

Three days had passed since they were locked in the dungeon.

One of the prisoners began showing symptoms.

“Uaaaaaaargh!”

He let out a monstrous scream and pounded on the door, but it must not have been an ordinary iron door, for it did not break. His face could be seen through the small window. Blood was flowing from his eyes and nose. He had lost his reason and was thrashing about madly.

Since the prisoners did not know that the reason they had been dragged here was Berserk, they thought he had simply been unable to withstand the confinement and had gone mad.

“You crazy bastard! Stop it!”

“Shut up! Do you want to die?”

But he only thrashed about even more madly.

Seeing that, the Poison King grew angry.

-That third-rate poison hack sold a drug like this for money?

Perhaps his ears were not ordinary, because Jindok Geosa soon came and watched the madly rampaging man through the small window. The man rushed forward and slammed his face into the opening. His killing intent was exploding.

Bang! Bang!

He rammed the iron door with his head.

The sight of blood streaming from his eyes, nose, and forehead was horrific, but Jindok Geosa’s gaze as he watched was only cold.

In the end, the prisoner rammed his head into the wall on his own and died on the spot.

The subordinates who had followed Jindok Geosa carried the corpse out with practiced ease, as if it were not the first or second time they had done so.

Jindok Geosa inspected the other dungeon cells as well. He examined the prisoners’ conditions one by one.

He looked at the room where Geom Mugeuk was. Geom Mugeuk sat leaning against the wall with his head bowed deeply. He revealed no particular aura.

After Geom Mugeuk, he looked into the Poison King’s cell.

The Poison King was sitting in the middle of the room and glanced toward the window.

The instant their gazes met, Jindok Geosa felt something different from the other prisoners.

‘Is he mocking me?’

Surely that could not be, but he did not like those eyes.

The Poison King lowered his head and cast his eyes down, and Jindok Geosa moved on to inspect the next room.

After looking through every room like that and turning to leave, Jindok Geosa looked into the Poison King’s room once more. This time, the Poison King had his back turned and was sunk in idle thought.

After watching him for a moment, Jindok Geosa left the place with his subordinates, and Geom Mugeuk immediately sent a sound transmission to the Poison King.

-How did it go?

-Why ask? Of course it succeeded.

-As expected! You are amazing.

In that brief encounter, the Poison King had poisoned Jindok Geosa with Berserk. Later, when the Sado Alliance examined the corpse, they needed to discover that he had been poisoned with Berserk. That was one of the main reasons we had infiltrated this place.

-To poison the foremost poison arts master of the unorthodox faction while looking him in the eye! Who in the martial world would dare succeed at such a thing? You truly are the Poison Sovereign!

-I only administered a minute amount for now, so it will not show. I will have to increase the dose little by little.

Though the Poison King spoke modestly, his expression said, “This much is nothing.”

Sensing that he wanted to boast more, Geom Mugeuk added praise.

-And from inside a dungeon cell to a target outside, no less. When I return, I will brag about it to everyone! I will tell my father of this magnificent sight. Father, the Poison King’s poison arts were art itself!

-There is no need to go so far as to tell the Cult Leader.

When Geom Mugeuk said he would tell his father, the Poison King seemed embarrassed yet quietly expectant.

Was that why? The Poison King was even more confident and dignified.

-I will poison him little by little each time he comes down.

Jindok Geosa might think he was conducting an experiment, but in truth, the subject of the experiment was himself.

* * *

When the second and third victims appeared in succession, the prisoners realized that something had been mixed into the food they were eating.

Only then did the prisoners stop eating.

At that, Jindok Geosa appeared again. He walked slowly down the dungeon corridor and spoke.

“I hear there are people who are not eating?”

All sorts of curses had flown around, saying that they would tear him to death if he came down, but now no one dared step forward. The sight of Hyeolsa melting into bloody liquid had been branded too shockingly into their minds.

“If you leave food behind, you will die by my poison. Even if you beg me to kill you, I will show you no mercy.”

After swinging a merciless whip, he also tossed them a rotten carrot.

“If you survive here, I will not send you back to the dungeon. I will simply let you go. One or two of you might survive.”

No one believed those words.

But they were certainly effective words. In the despair of watching people die one after another, this rotten carrot would eventually become their only consolation.

At that moment, Jindok Geosa stopped in front of the Poison King’s dungeon cell. Looking inside through the small door, he said to his subordinate,

“Open this door.”

The subordinate opened the iron door of the Poison King’s cell.

“Come out.”

The Poison King walked out of the dungeon cell. It was an unexpected situation that neither Geom Mugeuk nor the Poison King had anticipated.

Jindok Geosa looked at the Poison King and said,

“Why do I feel so unpleasant whenever I see you?”

He, too, was an absolute master of the evil path. It seemed he had sensed some ominous fate in his meeting with the Poison King.

With his finger, Jindok Geosa unpleasantly lifted the Poison King’s chin.

“For some reason, I do not like the look in your eyes.”

Even at this moment, the Poison King did not look frightened at all.

Poison King, put on a frightened expression! Even if he sent such a sound transmission, there was no way a man who had never made that expression in his life could make it now. Especially when he was already as displeased as he could be.

Jindok Geosa slowly raised his hand as if to slap him across the cheek. The Poison King’s lips twitched. Don’t, unless you want to die. It was a hair-trigger moment when words like those seemed about to come out.

Geom Mugeuk shouted through the small window.

“It is because the two of you resemble each other.”

The Poison King and Jindok Geosa looked toward Geom Mugeuk at the same time. One spoke with words, the other with an intense expression.

“What? We resemble each other?”

Chapter 223: Dealing with Poisoners Is Always Like This.
--------------------------------------------------------
Were the two of them alike?

There was not a single point of resemblance between them—so little that the Poison King would have been justified in leaping to his feet in outrage.

It was only something I had blurted out on the spur of the moment to turn Jindok Geosa’s attention toward me.

And I had achieved my purpose. Jindok Geosa’s interest shifted from the Poison King to me. Yes, deal with me.

“When you see someone who resembles you, doesn’t it put you in a bad mood for no reason?”

The look in the Poison King’s eyes when he heard that said this:

Me, resembling that ugly bastard?

Jindok Geosa seemed to have the same question.

“Just where do you think we resemble each other?”

Even he must have known they looked nothing alike.

“To my eyes, you resemble each other quite a lot. Even your gazes and the air about you are the same. Rebellious somehow, yet with depth.”

Jindok Geosa frowned. That expression said: You’re saying this nonsense in a situation like this? Rebellious? Depth? Here, in this underground prison where people are dying?

“Were you not that handsome when you were young?”

What was there to be offended about, being told he resembled a handsome man?

The problem was that he did not believe a word I said.

“Enough nonsense. Tell me why you stepped forward to save this man.”

Jindok Geosa openly revealed his killing intent. His stance made it clear that if I truly spouted nonsense, he would poison me as an example.

“He helped me in the prison.”

“How much help did he give you, for you to risk your life?”

My gaze turned to the Poison King.

“When I was fighting like a dog, that man became a dog with me and fought at my side.”

Because he had not expected me to give that reason, surprise and emotion flashed across the Poison King’s face.

Fortunately, more than any lengthy explanation, those words worked on Jindok Geosa.

“Prisoners talking about loyalty? If you stick your neck out insolently one more time, know that you’ll vanish into a handful of bloody water.”

“Yes! I’ll be careful.”

Jindok Geosa looked back and forth between me and the Poison King with displeasure, but he did not escalate the matter further. Each and every one of us was an important test subject.

Jindok Geosa locked the Poison King back in the prison, then left.

I sent a sound transmission to the Poison King.

—You endured well.

—Why did you step in? I could have handled it myself.

—I know.

—You did it because you thought I’d cause trouble, didn’t you?

—No.

—Don’t lie!

After a brief pause, I told him.

—If anything, I did it because I thought you might endure it.

—!

The Poison King looked at me with a startled expression.

—Because I thought you might endure it. I thought you might take a slap, suffer humiliation from that bastard, and still endure it. You probably would have endured for the sake of our plan, Poison King.

The Poison King’s clear, large eyes grew even wider.

—What if your flattery about me resembling him hadn’t worked and that bastard had tried to kill you?

—Then we’d have to overturn the board. We’d make it look as if that bastard killed everyone here and died from the side effects of Gwangpok, then leave.

—Then what about Aecha? He holes up in his lair and barely ever comes out.

—If we think about it, we’ll find a way.

—The reason you took even that kind of risk was… just because you didn’t want to see me suffer?

—Just? That’s a truly important reason. Important enough to overturn everything and go back.

At his gaze, which seemed to ask if this was more flattery, I revealed my true feelings.

“Between flattery and sincerity, the share of sincerity keeps growing.”

The Poison King, who had been staring at me, disappeared from the small window.

* * *

Prisoners died every day.

Each time, Jindok Geosa came to the prison to confirm their deaths, then glared at the Poison King before leaving. It seemed the Poison King’s existence still felt like a thorn in his eye.

He should have trusted his instincts more. He should have wondered why those clear eyes grated on him so much.

But he was exhausted from repeated failures in his research, and he failed to properly heed the sense of danger that kept sending him warnings.

On the contrary, he was being poisoned by Gwangpok every day. The Poison King administered the poison with such exquisite and outstanding skill, and since Jindok Geosa had no reason to use his internal energy violently in daily life, he could not perceive it.

After he left, the Poison King asked me.

—If things go according to our plan, will Aecha appear here?

—He definitely will. According to Strategist Go’s investigation, Aecha and Jindok Geosa don’t get along. If he hears the experiment succeeded, he’ll come in person to confirm it.

—Judging by the current pace, we’ll have an answer within about ten days.

The end was now in sight.

* * *

For ten days, I focused solely on the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

Rather, when our surroundings are chaotic, we sometimes display unexpected concentration. That was how I was. In this cramped, miserable prison where prisoners died every day, I concentrated on my training more than ever before.

And at last, there was progress in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

It was the result of the changes in my body caused by my recent increase in internal energy, combined with my training here. Slowly but steadily, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art was advancing toward great completion.

Once it rose by one stage, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art was clearly different from before. It responded to danger faster and more sensitively, and it felt even more closely bound to me.

Meanwhile, prisoners continued to die.

From the conversations of the martial artists who came and went bringing food to the prison, I heard that if this attempt failed, they planned to continue the experiment even if they had to abduct ordinary martial artists.

For the sake of their own goal, these people cared nothing for human lives. No, from the start, they were people who did not have others’ lives in their eyes at all.

These were the absolute evil. In a place like this, with masters like Jindok Geosa and Aecha conducting experiments in secret, who could possibly stop them, and how?

—Stopping such evil deeds is my Demonic Path.

And after I said those words, for the first time, I was asked a question about the next generation.

The Poison King asked me.

—After you die? Will the next Heavenly Demon truly be able to uphold that Demonic Path? I’m asking whether another person like you can appear.

—Why should I worry about what happens after I die? The people left behind will handle it themselves.

—You suffered this much to establish a new Demonic Path. Wouldn’t it be a waste if later generations ruined it?

Yes, they might ruin it. But I thought that was something I could do nothing about. No, from the start, I believed it was right not to interfere.

—Even if I ordered later generations, would that work? If I asked them, would they listen? They’ll simply watch how we live. If it looks admirable, they’ll follow it. If they think it’s not much, they’ll change it.

Just as I did.

* * *

As the Poison King had predicted, on the tenth day, every prisoner who had come here died.

Now the only people left were Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King.

Food continued to be provided for several days afterward, and naturally, the two did not die.

Then, several days after that.

The moment the two had been waiting for arrived.

At last, Aecha came to the underground prison with Jindok Geosa. Aecha, who had been living in seclusion in his own lair, showed himself for the first time.

“You truly succeeded?”

“I did! See for yourself. Two successful formulas came out. With further research, we can make them into even more stable formulas.”

“Let me have a look!”

Aecha peered inside through the iron door openings of the cells where Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King were kept.

His delighted eyes turned into those of a snake.

“They’re both too young. Is it not something that only works on young people?”

For a moment, Jindok Geosa almost cursed aloud. Aecha was always like this, but he had not expected him to pull this nonsense even today.

“Are you looking down on me too much?”

A cold wind blew between the two of them.

“I only said it in passing. There’s no need to bristle like that.”

After that hateful remark, Aecha once again looked back and forth between Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King inside the cells.

‘No matter how I look at them, they seem too young.’

At that moment, Aecha’s heart began to pound. His sense of danger was operating fiercely. What is it? What is this ominous feeling?

Like a God of Gamblers switching cards in front of a Gambling Ghost, the supreme essence of the Poison King’s martial arts was displayed in the presence of the foremost master of poison arts in the unorthodox faction. A single small hole was enough.

‘!’

Aecha’s expression stiffened for an instant. Forcing a smile, he approached Jindok Geosa.

“You truly worked hard!”

The instant he drew close.

Shhhk!

Aecha drew his sword with lightning speed and stabbed Jindok Geosa in the arm.

Caught by this surprise attack, Jindok Geosa was helpless.

The moment Jindok Geosa reflexively tried to scatter poison, Aecha suppressed his vital point one step ahead of him.

“Have you gone mad?”

At Jindok Geosa’s shout, Aecha seized him by the throat.

“What are you? Did you take Gwangpok?”

Jindok Geosa thought Aecha had gone berserk from Gwangpok. Otherwise, there was no reason he would suddenly try to kill him.

Nor could he be discarded now that the experiment had succeeded. To prepare for something like that, only he knew how to make Gwangpok. He had not written it down anywhere. It existed only in his head. Aecha knew that, so there was no reason to kill him.

“The antidote! Hand over the antidote!”

Aecha suddenly vomited blood.

His face had turned pitch-black. Only then did Jindok Geosa realize he had been poisoned. Aecha had mistaken him for the one who had poisoned him and attacked.

“You bastard! I knew you’d betray me like this someday!”

“It wasn’t me! I said it wasn’t me!”

If the two had normally been on good terms, they might have calmly examined their surroundings. But because they were as hostile as enemies, each could only think that the other was trying to kill him right now.

Just then, the subordinates of Aecha and Jindok Geosa, who had been waiting outside, rushed in.

“What happened?”

“Jindok betrayed us! Kill them all!”

Aecha’s subordinates drew their swords and stabbed Jindok Geosa’s subordinates to death. It happened in an instant, and because they were shocked by the words that their leader had betrayed them, Jindok Geosa’s subordinates could not immediately scatter poison and counterattack.

After all of them died, Aecha’s subordinates rushed toward Aecha, only to collapse one after another.

Aecha thought Jindok Geosa’s subordinates had used poison, but in truth, Geom Mugeuk had sent finger winds to press their acupoints and put them to sleep. They were the finest witnesses to explain this situation.

Both the earlier poisoning and the pressing of acupoints now had been done from inside the prison, through the small window, without drawing notice.

At the sight of his subordinates collapsing, Aecha’s eyes rolled with fury.

“You treacherous bastard! Hand over the antidote before I kill you!”

Aecha seized Jindok Geosa’s throat even more fiercely. With only a little more force, it would break. Unable to breathe, Jindok Geosa rasped as he said to him:

“If I had truly intended to kill you, you would already be dead.”

Because that much was true, Aecha was momentarily confused. Yes, if he had meant to kill me, he would have used a stronger poison and killed me instantly, wouldn’t he? He wouldn’t have allowed me to counterattack like this either.

It was right then.

Srrrk.

The key hanging at the waist of one of Jindok Geosa’s fallen subordinates rose into the air.

Jindok Geosa, who saw it first, widened his eyes.

The key flew across the air and opened the Poison King’s iron door.

Clack!

Startled by the sound, Aecha also looked that way.

The key flew back across the air and opened Geom Mugeuk’s iron door.

At the clack, Aecha and Jindok Geosa’s hearts sank.

As the doors opened, Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King walked out of the prison.

Aecha was dazed for a moment. Was he hallucinating because of the poison?

Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King walked side by side toward the two men.

Jindok Geosa was the first to grasp the situation.

“Kill them! They’re the ones who did this!”

Aecha came to his senses at once and thrust his sword toward Geom Mugeuk.

Even if he had lain in wait and launched a surprise attack, it would not have worked. How could it work now? Aecha’s thrust stabbed futilely through empty air, and Geom Mugeuk, who had dug in with the Yama Step, suppressed Aecha’s vital point.

Aecha looked at Geom Mugeuk with a shocked expression. Only now did it feel real. Things had gone wrong—terribly wrong. He should not have subdued Jindok Geosa and stabbed his arm.

The Poison King strode over and opened the poison pouch hanging at Jindok Geosa’s waist. At that moment, Jindok Geosa inwardly cried out in delight.

You dare touch my poison pouch so carelessly? Good, hurry up and die!

Of course he would die. If it could be handled carelessly, how could it be Jindok Geosa’s poison pouch?

But his opponent handled that poison even more skillfully than he did.

Riding a strand of wind, Jindok Geosa’s poison flew toward Aecha’s face.

“No!”

Aecha’s desperate cry soon turned into a horrifying scream.

“Aaaaaaaaargh!”

Because it was a signature poison used only by him, the one who killed Aecha would be Jindok Geosa.

Then who would kill Jindok Geosa?

Aecha’s hand, which held the sword, moved on its own by an invisible force.

Thud!

It buried itself in Jindok Geosa’s chest.

“Aaaaaaaaargh!”

“Uuuuuuuuugh!”

The two men screamed in agony at the same time.

Geom Mugeuk went over, jabbed his finger hard beneath Jindok Geosa’s eye, and flicked his nose.

Blood began to stream from his nose and eyes. There was already more than enough Gwangpok in his body, thanks to the Poison King’s poisoning; it would be concluded that the side effects of Gwangpok had made Jindok Geosa run amok, and that the two had ultimately destroyed each other.

“……Why on earth?”

At Jindok Geosa’s strained question, the Poison King answered curtly.

“Because you made medicine like a dog.”

Geom Mugeuk added nothing to that. He did not say that because of his medicine, blades had been drawn and many innocent people had died. He did not say that a child who had lost his family had been left all alone. Those words would never have gotten through to him. They would not have left even the smallest scratch on his conscience.

But to a man so full of pride, the Poison King’s words must have been a grievous wound.

This time, Aecha, half his face melted away by poison, asked Geom Mugeuk with difficulty.

“……Who are you? You people?”

Geom Mugeuk gazed at him silently, then said in a low voice.

“That gentleman is Gwangpok, and I am Heukbi.”

He had given the names of the two representative drugs they sold.

Aecha wanted to say something, but his life ended there. Even half a gak ago, he would never have imagined his life would end this emptily.

Watching Aecha’s head fall, Jindok Geosa said to the Poison King.

“……I have something to say.”

The Poison King went close to his side.

Jindok Geosa bit the inside of his cheek and spat blood. It was blood mixed with deadly poison.

Splash!

The Poison King unfolded the Muganseon and blocked it.

Blood trickled down the fan. Aside from Jindok Geosa’s blood, the fan bore many traces of battle. It seemed the Poison King left those marks as they were, without erasing or replacing them.

“It won’t work. Not the poison of a third-rate poison peddler.”

Played with to the very end, Jindok Geosa trembled with anguish and humiliation before his head finally dropped with a thud.

Geom Mugeuk silently looked down at the two dead men. This was not only meaningful because one of Yaryulhan’s four subordinates had been killed.

It was the moment countless lives that would have been lost to Gwangpok were saved, and the moment the vengeance of the boy who had lost his mother and younger sibling was fulfilled. He could now go to Seo Daeryong and tell him to tell the boy when he returned. Your revenge has been achieved. No evil works before our cult. So from now on, at least you should live happily. Their deaths held that meaning.

Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully to the Poison King.

“You truly worked hard.”

He had truly suffered in this cramped, stifling place. Geom Mugeuk himself had spent all his spare time training, but for the Poison King, who had lived amid the nature of the Thousand Poisons Forest, it must have been suffocating.

“Dealing with poison peddlers is always this exhausting and filthy.”

“I enjoyed it. Because I was with you, Poison King.”

“Stop releasing poison. It’s already enough.”

“Pardon?”

Leaving behind Geom Mugeuk, who looked as if he had no idea what he meant, the Poison King strode out first.

Just before Geom Mugeuk left the place, he sent out finger wind and released the blood points of Aecha’s subordinates, whom he had put to sleep earlier.

A short while later, amid the commotion behind them—Jindok Geosa had gone mad, he had betrayed them, inform the higher-ups—the two vanished from that place without a sound.

Chapter 224: This Time, I Will Serve You.
-----------------------------------------
Geukak Soma was lying on the roof, gazing up at the sky.

For someone who had always stared only at white walls, looking up at the sky like this was a very unfamiliar sight.

Just then, someone spoke beside him.

“Even when you look at the sky, you only pick out the white clouds, don’t you?”

At the familiar voice, the eyes of Geukak Soma behind his mask smiled brightly.

“Have you returned safely?”

“Thanks to you, I have.”

The person who had spoken to him was Geom Muguk.

Geukak Soma rose. A moment later, Dokwang entered the courtyard. Dokwang’s appearance always gave off a clear, fresh impression.

But this time, Geom Muguk had experienced for himself just how frightening a person he was, despite that bright and youthful appearance. His use of poison was an art, and the results were fatal. He was someone capable of reaching into the poison pouch of the Unorthodox Faction’s foremost poison arts master.

Sensing our gazes, Dokwang entered while snapping open his fan with a flourish and waving it languidly.

Geukak Soma stood and greeted him with a light clasp of his hands.

“You have worked hard.”

“Not at all. I merely followed the plan. It was an excellent plan.”

Geom Muguk spoke up.

“If not for Dokwang, we could never have accomplished this.”

He now understood clearly why his father valued Dokwang, and why the martial world feared him.

Dokwang looked up at Geom Muguk and asked,

“Are you going to say that to everyone you meet?”

Geom Muguk knew what he meant. This was what he wanted to hear.

“Yes. Until I tell my father one last time.”

Satisfaction flickered around Dokwang’s eyes at hearing the words he wanted.

With a snap, he folded his fan and went inside.

“Ah. It feels wonderful to see the sky after being in the prison.”

Geom Muguk sat down, while Geukak Soma remained standing where he was. Together, they looked up at the sky.

They remained that way for a while, until someone entered through the main gate and said,

“I’m back.”

Seo Daeryong had returned from the market with food. Both his hands and his arms were filled with ingredients for cooking.

“I’ll prepare the meal. Please wait just a little.”

Seo Daeryong spoke while looking at the ground.

A long green onion covered his face, but that did not mean he could not see them. He simply could not meet Geukak Soma’s eyes.

“There were a lot of fresh ingredients today. I even learned how to make a dish we haven’t had before from an inn’s cook. I’ll make it delicious for you.”

Geom Muguk watched Seo Daeryong ramble on without even making eye contact, smiling all the while. He had not even realized that Geom Muguk was there.

“Thank you, as always.”

At Geukak Soma’s words, Seo Daeryong’s gaze burrowed even deeper into the ground.

“It’s only natural. It’s what I should do. Ah, and I dreamed last night that you had returned, Hall Master. Our Hall Master has a talent for making people miss him without even realizing it.”

Geom Muguk spoke to him.

“I suppose I should have stayed away until you missed me desperately.”

Seo Daeryong was startled and raised his head.

“Hall Master!”

The ingredients tumbled from Seo Daeryong’s wide-eyed arms.

Whoosh.

The falling ingredients floated in the air, caught by Geom Muguk’s telekinesis. In the prison, it had been keys. Now it was green onions, vegetables, and meat.

Seo Daeryong hurriedly gathered them up.

Unable to hide his delight, Seo Daeryong held the food ingredients close. Geom Muguk could tell. If Geukak Soma had not been standing beside him, Seo Daeryong would have flown straight up to the roof and embraced him. He would have buried himself in the green onions.

“Go and tell that boy. We have taken our revenge, so he should go on living his own life.”

The instant he spoke, Seo Daeryong’s emotions surged. Throughout the entire journey, he had been thinking of that boy.

“Yes. I will be sure to tell him that this is the path of the future Heavenly Demon.”

Geom Muguk said with a smile,

“My brother might hear that.”

Seo Daeryong quickly corrected himself.

“I will be sure to tell him that this is the path of the Second Young Master.”

Geom Muguk leaped down from the roof.

“Let’s eat. I’m hungry. But are you crying?”

“Crying? The onions are just too strong.”

Seo Daeryong went inside with the unpeeled onions.

Before following him in, Geom Muguk looked up at Geukak Soma, who was still standing on the roof.

Their eyes met, and the two exchanged a slight nod.

Geom Muguk always ate separately from Geukak Soma, but he always met his eyes like this before every meal. It made him feel as if he were eating together with him.

* * *

That evening, Gowol and the Pungcheon Cult Master joined them.

“Young Master!”

“Second Young Master!”

The two of them were as unchanging as ever.

“The filthy and dangerous medicine will no longer be circulating.”

The Pungcheon Cult Master praised Dokwang and me for our efforts.

“You have worked hard as well, Dokwang.”

“It was a good experience.”

“It must have been interesting.”

The Pungcheon Cult Master was secretly envious. He had devoted himself so thoroughly for my sake that I thought I should set aside some time to spend with him separately as well.

After exchanging greetings, we all sat around together.

While we were dealing with Aech’a and Jindok Geosa, Gowol had already finished investigating our next target.

“Our next target is Lü Bugai.”

Before he began his explanation, I asked about something we needed to clarify first.

“Two people have died one after another. Won’t Yelü Han become suspicious?”

“He certainly will. But what can he do? There are numerous subordinates who witnessed the two of them fighting with drawn blades. Moreover, one of them was poisoned by Gwangpok. In the end, he will have no choice but to think that misfortune simply piled upon misfortune.”

“There is no chance that he will summon the remaining two?”

“No. Besides, Yelü Han is the sort of man who detests yielding to external pressure above all else.”

Taking it a step further, he continued,

“Since what happened has already happened, he will simply search for someone to fill the vacant position. He is not a man who looks back.”

“There will be plenty of people. Unorthodox masters must be lining up to join hands with the Second-in-Command of the Unorthodox Alliance.”

At that, the Pungcheon Cult Master stepped in.

“If Lü Bugai dies as well, he will think differently. He will not believe that coincidence could occur three times.”

At those words, Gowol presented his plan for the next operation.

“So I propose that we eliminate Yelü Han before he learns of Lü Bugai’s death.”

“What of the last remaining member of the Quartet?”

“He is always at Yelü Han’s side. We will have to deal with him at the same time regardless.”

Kill Lü Bugai, then eliminate Yelü Han and his final subordinate before news of Lü Bugai’s death spread. In other words, he was suggesting that we deal with all three almost simultaneously.

“Good. We’ll do that.”

I accepted his proposal without hesitation.

“Thank you. I will submit the detailed plan shortly.”

Dokwang, who had been listening, suddenly asked,

“Once Yelü Han is dead, won’t the Unorthodox Alliance suspect that we were responsible?”

I answered in Gowol’s place.

“I have one solution to that problem. I don’t yet know whether it will work, though.”

Dokwang looked at me as if asking whether I could truly accomplish something like that. In truth, I did not know either. I could not know what events awaited us.

“First, tell me about Lü Bugai.”

Gowol explained.

“Lü Bugai is a man with a rat tattoo on his chest. He manages hundreds of pleasure houses throughout the Central Plains and is known as the very emperor of the night. The reason he bears a rat tattoo is to display his determination to make money at night without sleeping.”

“What is his personality like?”

“He is an utterly vicious man as well. He abducted women, took the daughters of families driven to the brink by the Yama Stockade, and employed every foul means imaginable to build the city that never sleeps he controls today. His lust is also extraordinarily strong, and countless women have suffered at his hands. There is even a rumor that he sells aged courtesans’ bodies to those who practice bizarre unorthodox arts.”

If Jisaeng had been fated to die because of Sinseonchae, and Aech’a and Jindok Geosa had deserved to die because of Gwangpok, then this man was one who deserved death as well.

Having received my father’s permission to deal with both Yelü Han and the Quartet, I intended to eliminate every last one of them. If not for me and the Demon Lords, who could possibly bring down the man who had risen to become the emperor of the night—a man protected by the Second-in-Command of the Unorthodox Alliance?

“He is expanding his pleasure houses throughout the Central Plains, and recently he has been trying to extend his territory into Guizhou.”

That was when Geukak Soma opened his mouth.

“Did you say Guizhou?”

“That is correct.”

Geukak Soma’s gaze turned cold.

There was a Cheonhwa Pavilion in Guizhou. The woman who loved Geukak Soma, Ye Jeong, the mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion, was there as well.

Geukak Soma looked at me. Without another word, I told him,

“This time, I will attend you, Soma-nim.”

Geukak Soma nodded, then rose and walked out.

Everyone looked at me, wondering what was happening, but it was not my place to explain.

“We will depart for Guizhou early tomorrow morning. Everyone, prepare yourselves.”

* * *

After the meeting, I had a cup of tea separately with Gowol.

“We need to be even more careful this time.”

I told Gowol everything about Cheonhwa Pavilion, its mistress Ye Jeong, and her relationship with Geukak Soma. Everyone else might remain unaware, but the strategist had to know.

“The net cast by Heaven truly lets nothing slip through.”

I silently nodded.

After learning that Geukak Soma had a deep relationship with Cheonhwa Pavilion, Gowol grew concerned.

“If anything goes wrong, our identity could be exposed.”

“Soma-nim knows that well too. There is no need to worry so much.”

“When someone they care about becomes involved, unexpected variables can arise.”

“There will be no variables. You can trust him.”

At my certainty, Gowol smiled.

“Why are you smiling?”

“I was wondering whether there was anyone you trusted this much, Young Master.”

“You’re here, aren’t you? Investigator Seo as well. Ian, Lord Jang too. I trust them all equally.”

Even so, it seemed he could sense that I regarded Geukak Soma with a different kind of feeling.

“May I ask you one question?”

“What is it?”

“Have you never considered that the people you just mentioned might betray you?”

He seemed concerned because I continued forming such deep relationships with people.

“Deliberate betrayal isn’t all that common, is it? Unless someone is persuaded or coerced by another, don’t most people simply come to feel betrayed for one reason or another as they live their lives?”

Gowol nodded and listened intently.

“Even the feeling of betrayal can be understood differently depending on one’s position. Someone may leave because they have been disappointed in the other person, but simply leaving can be enough for them to be branded a traitor. Perhaps the person sending them away is the real traitor.”

I shared my convictions about parting with people.

“If someone wants to leave me, I will send them off with even greater courtesy than I showed when welcoming them. I will not turn someone who leaves into a traitor.”

A smile appeared on Gowol’s face.

“At times like this, you seem more like an elder brother than I am.”

“That’s because I’m an old soul in a young body.”

I needed to have many sincere conversations with Gowol. The more accurately he understood what kind of person I was, the better a strategist he would become. If I simply hoped for him to become a good strategist without making such efforts, that would be when a traitor was created.

Just then, someone appeared before us.

“Can I join you?”

It was the Pungcheon Cult Master.

“Of course.”

He joined us.

“Come to think of it, you seem to have lost some weight.”

The Pungcheon Cult Master’s expression brightened as if he had been waiting to hear those words.

“Someone has been nagging me endlessly. I had no choice but to lose it. A man ought to have a solid, dependable charm, after all.”

Gowol, who had been so serious in front of me, showed a different side of himself.

“The Cult Master used to be fat.”

“I know. That’s why I’m training so hard!”

Given the nature of the Pungcheon Cult Master’s martial arts, he barely moved, which had caused him to put on even more weight.

“You need to lose more. You still have a long way to go.”

“Enough with the nagging! Bring me liquor and meat! I’m going to eat it all tonight!”

If one heard only this conversation, who in the world would think this man was the Pungcheon Cult Master? Who would think he was the shackled man from back then?

Seo Daeryong joined us as well.

“How was it? What was it like with Soma-nim?”

“He was frightening.”

“It seemed like he was more than just frightening.”

“What do you mean?”

“When you came back from the market with the food, you were talking quite a lot.”

“Don’t you know that people talk more when they’re frightened?”

Still, it seemed Geukak Soma had been more than merely frightening.

“To be honest, I’m a little disappointed too. Whenever I brought meals to Soma-nim, my heart would pound so loudly I could hear it in my ears. That strange tension—it’s addictive.”

Then another voice spoke from behind him.

“Addiction is my specialty.”

When I turned around, it was Dokwang.

“Why are four men gathered together drinking tea?”

He sat down while making a pointless remark, despite not even liking alcohol that much.

“Our Investigator Seo was just telling us about becoming addicted to Soma-nim.”

“It couldn’t have been easy to keep Soma in a good mood.”

Taking it as his opportunity, Seo Daeryong reported to us.

“I wasn’t going to mention this, but… Soma-nim has a rather small appetite, despite how he looks. He doesn’t like eating the same food two meals in a row. So every time, I have to wander the market as forlornly as a wolf drenched by the rain, wondering what side dish to buy today and what dish to prepare for him tomorrow…”

That was when Seo Daeryong noticed that everyone’s eyes were focused behind him.

With a sorrowful expression, Seo Daeryong said,

“…I’m wrong, aren’t I? Please tell me I’m wrong.”

Geukak Soma sat down beside Seo Daeryong and said,

“I was raised delicately, so I have a small appetite.”

Seo Daeryong lowered his head deeply.

“I have a big mouth, though. Please kill me.”

“My life has grown shorter by as much as my appetite, so it’s all right.”

At Geukak Soma’s joke, Seo Daeryong clutched his head, and everyone laughed as they watched him.

Geukak Soma did not look offended at all. Rather, he seemed a little more cheerful and pleased than usual. And so I knew.

‘Lü Bugai, you’re in serious trouble.’

Chapter 225: A Distinguished Guest Will Be Coming Today.
--------------------------------------------------------
The next morning, before setting out, I went to see Geugak Soma.

“What is it?”

“Only Soma and I need to go to Cheonhwa House.”

The fact that Geugak Soma had a personal relationship with the Cheonhwa House Lord was an exceedingly private matter. I believed it was a secret that ought to be protected.

But Geugak Soma gave me an unexpected answer.

“There is no need for that. Let us all go together and proceed with the matter according to the original plan.”

“You need not force yourself to do something you do not wish to do. Military Advisor Go and the others can cooperate from outside.”

“That is not the reason.”

There was an unexpected reason he wanted us to go together.

“I want to introduce you to the Cheonhwa House Lord.”

The Poison King, Seo Daeryong, Go Wol, and even the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“The Cheonhwa House Lord is an ambitious person. But because of me, she has been restraining herself from pursuing many of the things she wishes to do. Meeting and associating with these people will be of great help to her.”

I could tell that Geugak Soma genuinely cared for the Cheonhwa House Lord. And I could also tell that he looked upon her with feelings greater than mere affection.

“And above all, they are people Second Young Master Yi trusts, are they not? So it is fine.”

I thought Geugak Soma’s decision was wise. There were times when it was best to keep things well hidden, and times when it was best to bring them out into the world. This was likely the latter. At the very least, I did not believe any of us would bring harm to the Cheonhwa House Lord. We sealed the decision with a smile exchanged between us.

When I left Geugak Soma’s residence and entered the courtyard, Go Wol was preparing to depart, loading baggage into the carriage. He seemed to have many worries weighing on his face.

“Military Advisor Go. Do not worry too much—enjoy yourself. As a military advisor, you will have to devise countless plans throughout your life, but the chance for me, Soma, and the Poison King to carry out those plans will not come often. So enjoy this situation.”

Go Wol bowed his head with a grateful expression. The Pungcheon Cult Leader beside him did not neglect to offer a jealous remark.

“The points I earn with a hundred words, you always earn with just one.”

“Please trust Military Advisor Go. I will give you one point and the Cult Leader a hundred.”

Thus, after soothing both my one and only military advisor and the cult leader’s feelings, I shouted loudly,

“Now, we depart!”

* * *

It was a lavishly decorated room.

The specially made bed was large enough for ten people to lie down on with room to spare, and the furnishings filling the spacious room were expensive and precious, crafted by master artisans. The ceramics and paintings displayed throughout went without saying—even the doorknob was expensive enough to startle anyone.

In the middle of this vast and splendid room, more than ten women sat kneeling. They wore thin garments that revealed their flesh. Their ages varied, as did their beauty. But their expressions were all the same.

Despair.

They stared down at the floor with eyes containing all the despair in the world.

And seated in the center of the women lined up to either side was Yeo Bulgae. His hair was white and his face was deeply wrinkled, but his eyes still radiated passion no less intense than a young man’s.

Yeo Bulgae was reading a dispatch.

Not long after Jisaeng had died, news had arrived that Aech’a had died as well.

His wrinkled hand clenched the dispatch, crumpling it, and he threw it at the head of one of the women. The woman picked up the dispatch after it fell from her head and burned it in the oil lamp.

“What do you think of this?”

In response to his question, Hwang Yeom, seated directly in front of him, answered respectfully.

“They never showed it openly, but they were people who subtly looked down on us for dealing in women.”

In other words, he was indirectly saying that it was good they had died. Hwang Yeom was Yeo Bulgae’s right-hand man, and knew his master better than anyone.

Sure enough, Yeo Bulgae felt the same way.

Jisaeng and Aech’a had been insignificant people with nothing but pride, forever looking down on others. He had never once seen them greet him first. Therefore, what concerned him more than their deaths was this:

“I mean, what if someone from outside is attacking us?”

“It would be best to rule out that possibility.”

“Why?”

“According to the investigation, it is certain they died because of internal conflict. The higher-ups must have investigated with their senses fully alert as well. If someone had the ability to deceive them, would we still be alive?”

The wrinkles on Yeo Bulgae’s forehead deepened further.

“Are you anxious?”

“I could be the next target.”

He did not know that a single wish filled the eyes of the women bowing their heads on either side of him.

Please, let it be him next!

“Just in case, I will summon every expert we can mobilize.”

Yeo Bulgae nodded. Normally, he liked to make this joke:

“Even the Yama of Hell wouldn’t accept me. He’d say I would dirty the place.”

But it was a joke he made only when he was certain he would not die. Now that he actually sensed danger, he reacted to death more sensitively than anyone.

“Just in case… contact the Yin-Yang Ghosts as well.”

Hwang Yeom flinched at the words “Yin-Yang Ghosts,” but soon replied obediently,

“Understood.”

In the past, such a matter would have required several exchanges. It would cost too much money to mobilize those men, and if they were unleashed upon the martial world for no reason, they might cause an incident. The higher-ups would not be pleased with that. But now, it was convenient that such deliberations could be skipped.

Hwang Yeom knew him so well that Yeo Bulgae sometimes felt as though he possessed another body. To put it simply, Hwang Yeom could identify the woman Yeo Bulgae had chosen from among dozens simply by looking at his master’s eyes.

“I am not afraid of dying, but I have yet to achieve all my dreams.”

Yeo Bulgae’s dream was to establish his pleasure houses throughout every corner of the Central Plains.

Until now, he had used every method imaginable to expand his pleasure houses across the Central Plains. Sometimes he built them himself, and sometimes he seized those belonging to others.

Every possible method had been employed in the process. He sent thugs to cause disturbances, spread groundless rumors, drugged the food and made the guests ill, and even set fire to the pleasure houses.

One might wonder whether such crude methods could work, but they were unexpectedly the most effective. He damaged their reputations, drove competing pleasure houses out of business, and bought up their establishments for next to nothing. Whenever he saw a pleasure-house owner take his own life in the process, Yeo Bulgae felt a rush of pleasure.

“You must cling to life and achieve your dream. Here, this is the new dream you will pursue, Lord.”

Hwang Yeom handed him a document. It contained information about Cheonhwa House. Since it was the most famous pleasure house in Guizhou, where they intended to expand, they had decided to seize it first.

As Yeo Bulgae looked through the contents, greed shone in his eyes.

“The projected profits are enormous.”

“It earns more money than any place we possess in the Central Plains.”

“Are the courtesans beauties?”

“That is part of it, but it is said the Cheonhwa House Lord is highly capable.”

“I heard the Cheonhwa House Lord is a woman?”

“That is correct. She handles the courtesans well, and she handles the guests well too. Even the martial artists under her command respect her.”

“Whatever she is, she is still just a worthless bitch.”

His contempt for women was an innate part of his nature. He despised and loathed them so intensely that Hwang Yeom suspected Yeo Bulgae had suffered severe abuse from his mother or some other woman in his youth. His pathological lust clearly stemmed not from affection, but from hatred.

“How beautiful is she?”

“I hear she is exceptionally beautiful.”

Yeo Bulgae’s eyes grew languid as his lust surfaced. His gaze turned toward the women. All of them had once been pleasure-house owners. To the outside world, they were believed to have disappeared or died.

They were women who had handed their pleasure houses over to Yeo Bulgae one morning and vanished somewhere. No matter how desperately their families and friends searched, they had never been found.

Yeo Bulgae had been abducting and collecting the more beautiful women among the owners of the pleasure houses he seized. It was a deed that would make Heaven and humanity tremble with outrage, but the truth had never become known outside. Even if someone had learned of it, no one dared face Yeo Bulgae.

“Now the balance will finally be right.”

There were nine women in all. He meant that when the Cheonhwa House Lord arrived, there would be ten.

Hwang Yeom nodded with an expression that told him not to worry. It was a signal that once the matter was finished, he would send the Cheonhwa House Lord to Yeo Bulgae’s chamber.

“The Black-and-White Twin Blades have already gone.”

The Black-and-White Twin Blades were Yeo Bulgae’s hands and feet, though they were not well known in the martial world.
